Grenada \b............................................................................................................1044 Grenada\b ............................................................................................797 Trinidad and Cayenne\b ...............................................................517 S Martenique and Saint Vincent\b .......................................596 Saint Lucia\b....................................................................................1273 World ARC Suggested Books Atlantic Pilot Atlas ISBN 9781408122471 by James Clarke. Large format map-book showing wind and current percentage information for every month of the year. It covers the Atlantic, Caribbean and Mediterranean. There is good commentary on each month too. Atlantic Crossing Guide ISBN 9781472947666 by RCC Pilotage Foundation and Jane Rusell. A useful book to read if you have not cruised the Atlantic before. Introduces all the basic problems, as well as giving outline port information. Chris Doyles Sailors Guides Chris Doyle’s Sailors Guides are regularly updated, accurate and good value. They make navigating straightforward. He provides sailing, mooring, shopping, eating, leisure, as well as technical support. The range includes Windward Islands, Grenada to Martinique, Leeward Islands, Anguilla to Dominique, Trinidad and Tobago, Venezuela and Bonaire. Useful reading before or after the Rally. https://doyleguides.com/ Caribbean Cruising: Your Guide to the perfect sailing holiday ISBN 9781574091724 by Jane Gibb and John Kretschmer. Full of top tips for your boat and equipment preparations. Highly recommended.\nGrenada to the Virgin Islands ISBN 9781846235818 by James Patuelli. Good detail on ports to visit while cruising.\nThe Panama Cruising Guide By Eric Bauhaus ISBN 9789962023296. ‘The Panama Bible’. Great colour maps and aerial photographs of the San Blas islands and there is a good section on transiting the Canal.\nSouth Atlantic Circuit By Tom Morgan ISBN 9789541900338 A comprehensive reference to Brazil, Uruguay and Argentina, the South Atlantic Islands, South Africa and Namibia.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 5 © World Cruising Club 2026 Havens and Anchorages By Tom Morgan/RCC Pilotage Foundation ISBN 9780954190040 A companion guide to the South Atlantic Circuit covering smaller and less known destinations Brazil Cruising Guide By Michel Balette ISBN 9781846232015 Translated from French. Over 250 harbours and anchorages are described and illustrated in full colour with charts and photographs. Published in 2010.\nMarquesan for All: Te ‘Eo No Te Paotu By Emily Davidson 9798877450233 Short, accessible and easy to use guide to the Marquesan language that transcends three languages--English, French, and Marquesan. Based on some 20 years of research, it compiles the local words and phrases you need to get around, while also encouraging locals to speak their native (and endangered) language. It will also help to cultivate stronger relationships between travellers and locals. Courtesy Flags ● Saint Lucia ● Panama ● Ecuador ● France ● Marquesas Flag (not a legal requirement alongside the French flag, however some locals appreciate it being flown) ● French Polynesia Flag ● Niue ● Tonga ● Fiji ● Vanuatu ● Australia ● Indonesia ● Mauritius ● South Africa ● Namibia ● St. Helena ● Brazil ● Grenada Available from the online shop at worldcruising.com/ Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 6 / 03. Local Information Picture yourself cruising through azure waters, as gentle trade winds guide your vessel, and the sun kisses your face while you navigate the captivating beauty of Saint Lucia. As the proud host of the Atlantic Rally for Cruisers (ARC) for 36 years, the island is a firm favourite among the sailing community, and for good reason. Seafarers are drawn by the island’s spectacular scenic backdrops, rich cultural experiences, and array of unforgettable adventures on land and sea. Plus, with two first-class marinas, IGY Rodney Bay Marina and Marigot Bay Yacht Haven, thrilling regattas, an exclusive yacht club, and delectable cuisine, the island will effortlessly complement your passion for the sea. For inspiration call your travel agent or visit stlucia.org SAIL THE SIGHTS OF SAINT LUCIA Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 7 © World Cruising Club 2026 Saint Lucia Information Local Information Dialling Code: +1 758 Time zone: UTC -4 Currency: EC$ Emergency: 911 Police: 999 or 4359 Police and emergency services can also be contacted via VHF Ch 16 Vigie and Moule à Chicque lighthouses are manned 24/7. All monitor VHF Ch 16 Useful Websites: saintlucia.orgstlucia.org IGY Rodney Bay Marina P.O. Box RB 2521, Castries Saint Lucia West Indies Location:\t14°04’N 60°57’W Open: Mon-Fri: 09:00-14:00 & 16:00-19:00 Sat-Sun: 09:00-14:00 VHF: Channel 16 T: +1 758 458 7200 Email: RBM@igymarinas.com Website: igy-rodneybay.com Water: Potable Power 220V/50Hz & 110V/60Hz Entry Formalities On arrival, all yachts must clear into Saint Lucia as soon as possible - crew must remain onboard until this is completed: 1. Visit the Port Health Office in the marina complex. A form is required to be completed by each crew member. This form will be provided on arrival by the World Cruising Club. 2. Visit Customs and Immigration Office in the marina complex. Take boat papers and all crew passports. Yachts MUST have pre-registered with SailClear.com. Clearance papers are printed off for you in the office, which considerably speeds up the procedure. Yachtsmen are permitted to stay on the island for a period of 6 months. The Customs and Immigration office is open daily: 08:00-12:00 and 13:30-18:00. 3. Visit Rodney Bay Marina Office To confirm your berth location, as well as show ship’s papers and boat insurance documentation. They will then send someone to connect your electricity and water. On departure contact the marina on CH16 so that your meter can be read for a speed checkout 4. An immigration form must be completed for each family or boat no more than 72 hours before or on arrival. It can be found here: https://travelslu.govt.lc/ Please fill out the form before going to immigration.\nVisas A list of countries whose nationals need a visa for Saint Lucia can be found at: https://www.govt.lc/services/apply-for-saint-lucia-non-immigrant-visa. Crew arriving by air to join the boat in Saint Lucia must have a letter from the skipper confirming that they will be travelling aboard and details an exact date of departure. (See page 68 in the Preparations section) Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 8 / 03. Local Information Arriving with Pets You must declare any pets when you arrive in Saint Lucia and a visit from the vet will be arranged to approve whether or not the animal can come ashore. The pet is not permitted ashore until checked. See .govt.lc/services/veterinary-import-permit Please be aware that if the authorities are not contacted in advance, or the paperwork is not correct, the animal will be denied entry, not permitted ashore and may be euthanized. Once pets have cleared in, they will be given a tag or collar to wear at all times while not on the boat.\nMake sure you arrange an export permit when you are leaving Saint Lucia for any other port.\nIGY Rodney Bay Marina Marina Facilities IGY Rodney Bay Marina has 253 berths plus 37 superyacht slips. Water and electricity are available on every pontoon. There is a small shopping complex in the marina with a range shops including a supermarket, laundry, duty free liquor store, an IATA travel agent and car hire company. There is also a good selection of cafés and restaurants, and a small onsite swimming pool.\nBoat needs are well catered for by the marina boatyard, electronic needs by Regis Electronics and chandlery by Island Water World. All types of gas bottles can be filled locally and there is also a sail loft in the complex. The boatyard has a range of services available, including a 75-ton hoist and secure dry storage for up to 120 yachts. They can carry out fibreglass repairs and have a full mechanical and machine shop as well as day workers for cleaning, varnishing and odd jobs. See the A to Z of Boat Services from page 13 for more details.\nReception Opening Hours Mon-Fri: 0900-1400 and 1600-1900 Sat-Sun: 0900-1400.\nTeam Snow with their pet great dane A B D C A: Pigeon Island B: Anchorage (whole bay) C: IGY Marina Docks D: IGY Marina Mooring Buoys Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 11 © World Cruising Club 2026 Showers There are several toilet and shower blocks in the marina, including a large modern block close to the ARC Office. These are accessed with a code, which will be given to you on arrival.\nWater and Electricity Do not attempt to turn on your own water and electricity.\nOnce you have visited the Marina Office and paid for your berth, sign your meter rates and call the Rodney Bay Marina dock staff on Ch.16 to come and connect your electricity and water. It is suggested that you read meters along with our dock attendant to ensure accuracy in your metered charges for utilities.\nAs much as possible boats are berthed according to their needs. Please ensure your power requirements are added to your Boat Information via the members area. NOTE: It is always a good idea to have a range of different adaptors on board, including standard European 16, 32 and 50 Amp plugs with associated conversion necklaces, and splitters.\nPlease note you may have to share water connections, however, the dockmasters manage this process effectively. Anyone found to be taking water from another yacht’s tap may be responsible for the entire water cost of the metered yacht.\nDressing Your Boat All rally participants are asked to dress their boat with code flags whilst in harbour to create a festive atmosphere. Sets of code flags can be bought from the online shop at worldcruising.com/ Mooring Buoys in Rodney Bay The IGY Rodney Bay Marina offers mooring buoys in the lagoon next to the marina, for boat owners looking for a more economical option for long-term berthing. For more information, contact: rbm@igymarinas.com + 1 758 458 7200 Security All pontoons are gated, with a security guard on each gate. Ensure you lock you boat when leaving it unattended, and don’t leave anything of value on deck or on the pontoons overnight (including shoes).\nDinghy theft can be a problem throughout the Caribbean. Remember to padlock your dinghy and outboard at all times to the yacht, even when at anchor or in a marina. We recommend you lift your dinghy out of the water at night using a lifting strop and halyard, or lift it on deck. When leaving your dinghy ashore, always padlock it to a permanent structure using a wire that runs through the engine and fuel tank, with a padlock.\nPower Power supply varies by pontoon but is predominately 220V/50Hz with 110/60Hz at selected docks.\nWater Potable water is available on every pontoon. Rodney Bay Marina Security Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 12 / 03. Local Information Special Advertising Section ST. LUCIA, W.I.\nIGY Rodney Bay Marina & Boatyard F rom routine maintenance to complete refits, our full-service boatyard is geared up to get you sailing swiftly and safely. Featuring a 75-ton Ma-rine Travel Lift, metal fabrication, fiberglass experts, and a Sail Loft with skilled riggers and sail repair experts, we’re your go-to destination.\nEndorsed by major international insurance companies, our boatyard is a top choice for summer and hurricane season storage. Nestled within the IGY Rodney Bay Marina complex, our gated 4.5-acre haven in a protected lagoon is a designated port of entry, ensuring a breeze for clearing in and hauling out in a breeze.\nOnce settled in, the 5 Gold Anchor-awarded Rodney Bay Marina offers full support facilities. Explore further at Rodney Bay Village, a short distance away, with two shopping malls and a strip featuring bars and restaurants.\nOur professionalism, friendliness, and affordable rates make us stand out. St. Lucia’s central location makes us the perfect base for sailors exploring the Caribbean islands. With daily flights to major hubs in Canada (Toronto) and the US (Atlanta, Miami & NYC) as well as Europe (London), we boast the best international flight connections in the Windward Islands.\nSeeking a reputable boatyard in the southern Caribbean? Choose IGY Rodney Bay Boatyard for industry stalwart quality assurance. Your journey to maritime excellence begins here!\nOnsite Amenities Include: • Full-Service Boatyard & Technical Services • Customs & Immigration • 24 -HR CCTV Surveillance and Security • Complimentary Wi-Fi • Duty-free Fuel Station & In-slip Fueling • In-slip Water & Electricity (US & European power) • Floating and Concrete Docks • 8 Popular Restaurants & Bars • Extensive Provisioning- local, gourmet and international products • Bank, Laundromat, Showers, Taxi & Tour Agency & Car Rental • Hurricane Season Insurance packages through our Insurance Partners • Saltwater Pool • Special long-term contract deals year-round! P .O. Box 1538, Castries, St. Lucia WI Tel: +758.458.7200 www.igy-rodneybay.com rbm@igymarinas.com Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 13 © World Cruising Club 2026 A to Z of Boat Services There are a number of skilled and semi-skilled workers who operate in the marina and boatyard. Be aware of unskilled workers visiting your boat and offering their services. Get recommendations, or ask at the Marina Office for more details. Visit shopsatrbm.com/marine-services.html for a list of available services.\nBoatyard and Haulout Rodney Bay Marina Boatyard jmarius@igymarinas.com +1 758 458 7200 Open: Mon-Fri 08:00–17:00 with emrgency halout services available during the weekends and evenings. Offers a range of repair services. Haulout 75-ton lift; 27ft max. beam; 80ft. LOA. Contact the boatyard manager either through the marina reception or directly at the boatyard, situated at the northern end of the marina. Boat Vendors In every harbour and anchorage, you will likely be visited by enterprising locals driving a small boat, selling fresh fruit, bread and ice as well as offering tours and water taxis services. They can help with pilotage and, in some harbours and anchorages, the allocation of mooring. Putting fenders out along one side is a sign that you are happy for them to come alongside and it will also protect your boat. When engaging their services give them a list and agree a price in advance for each item. Find out their name and tell them to speak to the others indicating that there is no need for anyone else to call.\nChandleries Island Water World islandwaterworld.com +1 758 452 1222 Open: Mon-Fri 0830-1630 and Sat 0830-1230.\nLocated in Rodney Bay Marina. IWW has a comprehensive range of stock and extremely helpful and knowledgeable counter staff. With 7 branches across the Caribbean, they can usually find what you are looking for quickly and efficiently. Visiting World ARC participants who wish to purchase duty free, must take their original ship’s documentation and clearance papers with them the first time they shop. Johnsons Marine Centre info@ohnsonshardware.com +1 758 452 0303 Open: Mon-Fri 08:00-16:00 and Sat 08:00-13:00.\nOpposite the marina entrance. Whilst primarily a hardware store, they have an extensive chandlery section, and the costs are often lower than other shops’ duty-free prices. It is well worth a visit to compare prices. They also carry a full range of electrical items, including the same shore power plugs used at Rodney Bay Marina and 230-115 volt transformers.\nCharts and Pilot Books Island Water World and Johnsons Marine Centre (see above). Local pilot books can often be found in local souvenir type shops within the Rodney Bay Marina complex. Day Workers Rodney Bay Marina Freelancers +1 758 458 7200 For interior & exterior cleaning, engine room & general maintenance.\nA list of authorised freelancers is also available from the World ARC Office in Saint Lucia.\nElectronics & Electrical MarinTek marintek@gmail.com +1 758 484 60 31 / VHF Ch71 Rodney Bay Marina. Contact Egbert Charles. Regis Electronics stlucia@regiselectronics.com +1 758 452 0205 Rodney Bay Marina. Marine electronic and battery specialists (sales, service and installation). Alternators, batteries and battery chargers held in stock. They also stock spare parts and offer fast duty-free clearance for yacht spares in transit. Watermaker specialist.\nKenny from Rodney Bay Sails in Rodney Bay Marina Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 14 / 03. Local Information Fibreglass Rodney Bay Marina Boatyard (see ‘Boatyard’) Fuel (Diesel) Rodney Bay Marina Fuel dock is at the northern end of marina alongside the boatyard. Duty free prices are available after clearing out.\nOpen Mon-Fri: 08:00–17:00, Sat-Sun: 08:00-16:00 Gas (Cooking) Suds Laundry VHF Ch16 +1 758 286 7255 / 486 0718 Next to Dock J. Gas must be paid for in advance - only propane available. Cards accepted. 24hr turnaround. Suds can also adapt any connection fitting you may have on your gas bottle.\nGenerators MarinTek (see Electronics & Electrical). Local Information Positive Impact Fund s a il in g gl o b al ly - su p p or ti n g l o c a l l y Supporting the shores that welcome us The Positive Impact Fund, launched by World Cruising Club in 2025, was created with a simple but powerful goal: to support communities and local charities in the destinations visited by our rallies around the world.\nAs sailors, we are privileged to experience some of the most beautiful and remote places on the planet. The Positive Impact Fund ensures that, wherever our fleets arrive, they leave behind more than memories — they leave a meaningful and lasting contribution.\nDuring the 2025–26 Caribbean sailing season alone, approximately £15,000 have been raised through generous contributions from World Cruising Club, past and present rally participants, and valued industry partners.\nThese funds directly support local initiatives, helping strengthen communities and create a positive legacy in the wake of each rally.\nThe Positive Impact Fund gives participants the opportunity to give back to the destinations that welcome them — reinforcing the spirit of responsible cruising and ensuring that our adventures have a lasting, positive impact long after the sails are lowered.\nTO FIND OUT MORE OR IF YOU WOULD LIKE TO DONATE PLEASE VISIT: worldcruising.com/positive-impact-fund Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information Travel Information Airport Hewanorra International Airport (code UVF) This is the main International airport and it is located at the south of the Island, 50 miles and a 90 minute taxi ride from Rodney Bay Marina.\nNote that travel time can increase significantly during busy traffic times, particularly at rush hour and during bad weather.\nHewanorra Airport +1 758 454 6644 hewanorrainternationalairport.com Regional flights operate from Saint Lucia’s second airport, George Charles Airport (code SLU), located in Castries. This is only around 25 minutes from the marina, but most international flights do not use this airport. It is about 90 minutes by taxi from George Charles Airport to Hewanorra Airport.\nInternational Airlines Saint Lucia is well connected by air directly from North America, Europe and the Caribbean through a wide range of regional and international airlines. Each airline provides convenient services directly from major international cities or from a number of gateways including Antigua, Barbados, Chicago, Miami, New York, Boston, Atlanta, Philadelphia, Toronto and Puerto Rico. Airlines serving Saint Lucia from Europe: British Airways ba.com Virgin virginatlantic.com Air France (via Martinique) airfrance.com Air Caraïbes (via Martinique) aircaraibes.com Airlines serving Saint Lucia from North America: Air Canada aircanada.ca American Airlines aa.com Delta Airlines delta.com Jet Blue jetblue.com WestJet westjet.com Caribbean Flights Regional flights between Caribbean islands are frequent, and generally operate from Saint Lucia’s second airport, George Charles Airport (airport code SLU), located in Castries, about 25 minutes from the marina. georgeflcharlesairport.com +1 758 452 1156 Airlines serving George Charles Airport include: InterCaribbean Airways intercaribbean.com Air Caraïbes aircaraibes.com Caribbean Airlines caribbean-airlines.com Air Antilles airantilles.com Sunrise Airways sunriseairways.net Airport Transfers Approximate taxi costs one-way from Rodney Bay to Hewanorra (90 minutes): US $85 (up to 3 passengers); for 4 or more US$25 per person. St Lucia Reps dutyoffice@stluciareps.com +1 758 456 9100 suntourscaribbean.com Offer private and shared minibus transfers and are based in the Sun Group Building opposite the marina on the slip road. Water taxis also offer transfers, taking approximately 90 minutes, and charge around US$150 per person.\nBuses Local buses can be used to get nearly anywhere at very little cost. They have green ‘M’ registration plates. Buses pass outside the marina gates to/from Castries. Simply flag down any bus with a “Route 1A” sign.\nCar Rental Sixt Car Rental +1 758 452 9404 In the Marina, next to Customs & Immigration on the first floor of the marina building. Another car hire company on the island (that will likely charge for delivery to Rodney Bay) is: Paradise Car Rental +1 758 452 4934 You will need to purchase a temporary driving permit for Saint Lucia at the car rental office.\nRodney Bay Marina Office Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 25 © World Cruising Club 2026 Helicopter stluciahelicopters.com +1 758 453 6950 Travelling to the International Airport in the south this way means departing from George Charles airport in the north. With clearances, it will take about the same time as getting a taxi from the marina, but is a spectacular way to see the Pitons. Taxis Marina Taxi Stand +1 758 719 9877 Fully licensed taxi operators are denoted by blue ‘TX’ license plates. It is strongly recommended you use licensed operators and it is wise to agree on the specific fare before departing. The Taxi Association at Rodney Bay Marina Discounted fixed rates. +1 758 452 9957 Water Taxis These are an easy way to reach the beach, adjacent bars & restaurants or yachts at anchor.\nCall on VHF Ch 16, or can usually be found on the main pontoon.\nQuincy +1 758 716 6733 Shaka +1 758 721 2986 John +1 758 518 2290 Tafari +1 758 716 4004 Reliable Taxi Service provider for the ARC from it’s inception on St. Lucia for over 30 years.\nAirport Transfers, Shuttle Services, Island Tours, Shopping and Supply Services all by Ministry Of Tourism Certified Taxi Drivers.\nT A X I A S S O C I A T I O N Taxi Stand at Rodney Bay Marina Gate Cell WhatsApp: +1 (758) 721 5205 | T: +1 (758) 452 9957 | E: igymarinataxi@gmail.com Caribbean Travel Tips ● Lucian people are known for being courteous and hospitable. Say “good morning,” “good afternoon,” or “good evening” when greeting people and on entering a store, restaurant etc. ● Make sure you try the local fruits and vegetables from the markets, stall holders will be happy to give you recipe ideas and cooking advice. ● Be patient, the Caribbean operates at a different pace. ● Be polite to boat vendors and other locals touting for business - a polite “no thank you, not today” with a smile, generally works well. ● Remember to ask for a receipt when you are asked to pay mooring fees anywhere in the Caribbean. ● Don’t leave hatches open when you leave the boat - it can rain without notice! ● Be security conscious, lock your main hatches, dinghy and outboard at night and don’t leave anything valuable on deck. ● Avoid walking anywhere to or from the marina in the dark - take a taxi. Don’t be tempted to stay too late at the Gros Islet Jump Up. It’s a good night out... but hang onto your pockets. ● Did you know that it is illegal to wear or carry any kind of army/camouflage print in several Caribbean countries, including St Lucia? You could be fined or imprisoned!\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 26 / 03. Local Information Panama Local Information Dialling Code: +507 Time zone: UTC -5 Currency: US$ (Balboa) Emergency: 911 Language: Spanish and English is widely spoken Useful Websites: visitpanama.com panamainfo.com General Panama is the gateway to the Pacific for the World ARC fleet. Before and after your canal transit, you will have the opportunity to explore the San Blas and Las Perlas Islands, as well as Colon and Panama City.\nPilotage Information Charts 1400 Approaches to Puerto Cristobal 3111 Atlantic entrance to Panama Canal 3098 Panama Canal Pilots Panama Cruising Guide (Bauhaus) Arriving with Pets For full details of arriving in Panama with pets see: embassyofpanama.org/travelling-with-pets-1 Entry Formalities Yachts arriving in Panama waters have 72 hours after arrival to clear in with Immigration and then with the Port Captain. Shelter Bay Marina has a Port Captain and Immigration Officers on site Monday to Friday (office located behind the marina building). When the skipper goes ashore to complete clearance procedures, all others must remain onboard until clearance is complete.\nSee further information on entry formalities for the San Blas Islands (see page 32).\nAll international clearances and paperwork required for the Canal transit will be organised by the World ARC agent. Visas Nationals from over 100 countries can enter Panama without needing a visa.\nQuite a number of nationalities (including the UK and most Europeans) get a visa-waiver to enter Panama. (USA and Canada will be given a 6-month entry stamp, all other visa-waiver countries a 3-month entry stamp.) You must also show proof of economic solvency (minimum of US$500) upon entering, have a minimum of three months of validity in your passport and proof of outbound travel.\nIf you do not qualify for a visa-waiver, you will need either an authorised visa, or a stamped visa, obtained from a Panama consulate or embassy. The approval process can take at least a month.\nTo find out more: embassyofpanama.org/visas Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 27 © World Cruising Club 2026 Approaches Berthing will be organised prior to your arrival based on your ETA. A daylight arrival is strongly advised, as although well buoyed, the main entrance channel is hard to follow at night. The dockmaster at Shelter Bay Marina works from 08:00-16:00 and gives essential advice on navigating the entrance over the VHF. Beware the shoal ground extending 200m east from the southern side of the marina entrance which is not accurately depicted on some charts. Facilities Shelter Bay Marina is a well serviced and managed marina in a quiet area of the approach to the Panama Canal. A former US Army base, it has undergone substantial redevelopment and alongside the marina there is an outdoor swimming pool, gym, hotel, bar, supermarket and a range of yacht services. The marina is close to the San Lorenzo National Park and the centre of Colón is very accessible on transport provided by the marina. A local cruisers net operates on Ch.77 at 0730 each morning. Water & Electricity Potable water is available on every pontoon. Panama runs on the American system of 110 volts but some 220 volt single phase outlets can be provided on the marina berths. The marina has a supply of step-up transformers – 60Hz only.\nMarina Security The marina is in a quiet location and security has not been a problem. The marina is located alongside an active Coast Guard base with vehicle access through their gate. There is a guard 24/7 and occasional security forces training activity in the vicinity of the Marina.\nMost incidents of theft are petty or opportunistic, but several serious robberies/attacks have been reported in anchorages around Panama with a number of armed attacks against yachts especially in the area around Puerto Linton and Portobello. Check Noonsite.com for up-to-date reports. Nowhere in Panama (including Guna Yala) should yachts anchor at night in remote areas without being accompanied by other yachts. Always lock up at night and arrange to have your boat cared for if you will be away.\nA Word of Caution Colón is a depressed economic area with social problems. Despite the construction of the Duty Free Trade Area, unemployment and poverty are evident, and crime is sadly a common occurrence. Visitors should be vigilant. Check points at road junctions are common near Colón. If you are travelling in a taxi or organised group, you should not encounter any problems. If you are travelling in a hire car, the driver should be prepared to present their passport and driving licence when asked.\nShelter Bay Marina Shelter Bay Marina, 30 Butner Street, Colon, Republic of Panama Location:\t09°22’N 79°56’W Open: Daily 08:00-16:00 VHF: Ch.74 T: +507 4333 581 (Marina Office) Email: info@shelterbaymarina.com Website: shelterbaymarina.com Water: Potable Power 110V/220V 60Hz Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 28 / 03. Local Information Deliveries FedEx, Mailboxes Etc. and DHL are the best choices, as UPS and the local postal service can be unreliable. For FedEx and DHL, address packages to Shelter Bay Marina. Use +507 4333581 (for Shelter Bay Marina). Most packages will incur a service or customs fee, which will be paid by the marina, then charged to your account with a 20% service charge.\nMail Boxes Etc mbe-ca.com Will take care of all paperwork, customs, clearance etc. for you and they will come to deliver the goods to your boat.\nPAKYA pakya.com info@pakyapanama.com Freight forwarder serving cruisers in Panama. Contact them direct in either Panama or Miami. Panama: +507 2361728 / Miami: +1 305 8631818 A to Z of Boat Services Boatyard and Haulout Shelter Bay Marina yard@shelterbaymarina.com 5 acre storage and work yard. The yard office is located in the hangar building beside the travel lift bay. Chandleries Shelter Bay Chandlery store@shelterbaymarina.com There is a chandlery onsite which stocks a small range of work yard accessories. It is a branch of a larger chandler based in Panama City, and can get stock items to order in a couple of days.\nMarine Warehouse marinewarehouse.net panama@marinewarehouse.net Large marine supplier with offices Amador and Bocas Del Toro. Wide range of marine services electronics, hydroylics, watermakers, air conditioning, refridgeration, welding and mechanics.\nFibreglass / GRP Repair Repairs can be undertaken by arrangement with the Yard Manager.\nFuel The fuel dock/barge is run by the marina and fuel dispensing can be booked in the marina office. Payment must be made in advance of fuelling, with credit given for overpayment.\nGas (Cooking) Refills of gas, of most types of regulator and size, can be organised by the marina office. The turnaround may be a couple of working days. Laundry A laundry room is available in the marina office out building with commercial washers and dryers; they also have an iron and ironing board for use. The machines take cash, and it costs $3 per wash/dry. A service wash and dry service is also available at a cost of US$4 per lb.\nDoctors / Hospitals Emergency ambulance: 911 Please ask at the Marina Office for advice and assistance with appointments with English speaking Doctors. Centro Medico Caribe centromedicodel+507 441 4420 Near the Free Zone on Colon. Walk-ins accepted Hospital Colón 4 Altos hospitalcolon.com+507 433 7400 Located in Colón, near the shopping centre. Accounts can be opened in the marina office with a credit card for the bar, restaurant and shop. The restaurant and mini-market take credit cards, although there is a minimum spend in the mini-market.\nPharmacy Pharmacy Itzel +507 6741 0879 Located in Colon. Open Mon-Sat: 07:00-22:00 Sun: 08:00-20:00.\nPost Office Postal facilities are available in Colón. Fedex local.fedex.com/es-pa/colon-city +507 4791002 Calle 4a & 3a Avenue France Field, Colón. Restaurants Dock Restaurant and Bar thedockrestaurant.com Located in the Shelter Bay marina complex.\nRubbish Recyling facilities available in Shelter Bay Marina Shopping & Supermarkets There is a small but well stocked convenience store in Shelter Bay Marina called the Dock Shoppe. They can also arrange deliveries.\nMalls Cuatro Altos Shopping Centre Within this centre is a hardware and electronic store, internet café, beauty salon, paint store, three restaurants and several department stores. Open daily 07:00-21:45.\nColón 2000 (Dos Mil) colon2000dutyfree.com Tax free shopping with over 60 stores and 30 restaurants. Open 11:00-20:00. Cost $5 to enter. Local Information / 31 © World Cruising Club 2026 Travel Information Airport Tocumen Airport Panama City (PTY) tocumenpanama.aero The nearest international airport to Shelter Bay is around 1½ - 2 hours drive away. Flights operate to the Caribbean, North and South America, and Europe with KLM, Lufthansa and Iberia.\nBuses Shelter Bay marina runs an air-conditioned shopping shuttle bus to and from Colón twice a day (except Sunday). Further details will be provided on arrival from Rally Control. There is also a reasonable public bus service between Panama City to Colón. The service (US$2.50, two hours, every 30 minutes) departs from the Albrook Bus Terminal in Panama City and the Central Bus Station, Colón. Take the white air-conditioned bus and not the local bus. It would be wise to book an onward taxi from the bus station to the marina for any arrivals.\nCar Rental Budget +507 4417162 / +507 4417161 Hertz +507 4413272 Both are located in the 2000 (Dos Mil) shopping centre in Colón.\nTaxis The marina has a list of reliable English speaking taxi drivers. A taxi from Colón to the marina is about US$25. A taxi ride within Colón is US$1-2. To/from Tocumen Airport (Panama City) is US$120 and the going rate to hire a taxi for the day is about US$15 per hour. It is a good idea to negotiate and agree a price before each taxi ride. Uber Is available throughout Panama and is a reliable and cost effective way to travel.\nDon’t Miss The Panama Canal Visitor Centres visitcanaldepanama.com There are two visitors centres: One at Miraflores Lock and at the other Agua Clara - Visitor Centre at Gatun (on the Caribbean side near Colon City). There is also the Panama Canal Interactive Centre, where visitors can learn about the history of the Canal and see how it was built.\nSan Lorenzo National Park Shelter Bay Marina is a short walk away from park entrance. The park is ideal for walking, jogging or biking and much of it is shaded. You may encounter troops of howler and whitefaced monkeys, sloths and abundant bird life.\nFort San Lorenzo ppysl.org/sanlorenzo This well maintained historic site is located on a bluff just above the entrance to the Rio Chagres. It is a pleasant six mile walk from the Marina (take water) or a short taxi ride. There are signs in English and Spanish explaining the history of the fort and the surrounding area.\nLa Granja lagranjapanama.com Located 1/2 hour from the outskirts of Colón, this working farm offers horseback riding, a zip line and kayaking. They also have a Palapa right on Gatun Lake available for BBQ’s. For more detailed information look for brochures in the marina lobby. Panama Canal Railway https://panamacanal.tours/packages/railway What used to be a good train service between Colón and Panama Cuty is now a tourist attraction offering a 3-hour historic trip. Embera Tour 2026 Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 32 / 03. Local Information San Blas Islands Prior to transiting the Canal, you have the option of exploring the islands of the San Blas archipelago which stretches along the Caribbean coast of Panama from the Golfo de San Blas nearly all the way to the Colombian border. San Blas are a series of 378 islands, of which, only around 49 are inhabited by the fiercely independent Kuna Indians. The Kuna run all the islands as an autonomous province, with minimal interference from the national government. They maintain their own economic system, language, customs and culture. The Kuna are famous for their arts and crafts. In particular, is the mola, made with rainbow coloured fabrics, emblazoned with fish, birds, jungle animals and geometric designs. At anchorage you can expect a visit from the locals, in their dug out canoes, some with sails, to sell these wares and fresh produce.\nPilotage Information UKHO Charts 2417 Harbours on the N Coast of Central America US Charts 26060 Puerto Cristóbal to Cabo Tiburón 26063 Punta San Blas to Bahía Concepción 26042 Bahía Concepción to Punta Brava Pilots: Panama Cruising Guide (Bauhaus) Navigation Lights There are none. Pilotage at night is not recommended. Tides Negligible with a range of less than 0.6m. Currents in the channels are generally less than a knot, usually going west.\nEntry Formalities The San Blas islands are part of the republic of Panama, although they are administrated by the Kuna Indian who impose their own fees for arrivals. Make sure you leave Saint Lucia with sufficient US$ cash to cover these costs.\nIt is possible to formally enter the Republic of Panama at Porvenir and this will give the Immigration clearance for a visit to the country. Port authority clearances need to be done in the nearest entry place and recommend Puerto Linton, Portobello or Colón. If cruising from the east, it is also possible to carry out clearances at Rio Diablo on the south east corner of the archipelago. It is likely that in each populated anchorage the local tax will be charged again. However, local tax will probably include a guided tour ashore. The formal arrival procedure is not strictly enforced. In the past some cruisers have visited the islands from the east, not cleared though Rio Diablo and then cleared in and then out at Porvenir or even waited until Colón Facilities Provisioning The San Blas should not be considered a provisioning stop and it would be better to have sufficient stock to last until Shelter Bay Marina.\nMoney Cash withdrawals and currency exchange is not available. Have sufficient US$ before you leave Saint Lucia, in small denominations to pay locals for anchorage fees, as change is unlikely to be given. Telephone Digicell is the only network with reliable service in San Blas Islands, but not working on hotspot. Mas Movil has a very good signal only in Porvenir area and is usable as a hotspot.\nWater Rainwater catchment systems provide the islands with water of variable quality.\nSuggested Itinerary From the east likely places to visit would be: Rio Diablo - Nargana The island of Nargana has a population who have decided to give up the traditional Kuna ways and the village is very different to the remainder of the San Blas Islands. There is a bank, a police station and even a jail. Shops sell a little more than elsewhere; you can get beer, Coca-Cola and basic provisions. Girls wear modern clothing and only a few still wear traditional Molas. San Blas - Deserted Island Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 33 © World Cruising Club 2026 Green Island Green Island is a quiet anchorage well protected by the island and reefs. It is a good place to stop for swimming and exploring the island. Holandeses Islands These islands, split into two and named East and West Holandes, are popular stops to anchor. For a yacht travelling from the east, the group is probably the most accessible of all the islands, if one foregoes strict adherence to the entry formalities. Lemon Cay Further to the west is East Lemon Cay. This anchorage is protected by reefs and islands. A few people live here and they are often able to supply fresh fruit and bread. A popular spot for coral diving and there is also a wreck.\nPorvenir In Porvenir there are immigration facilities but no boat clearlances are possible. It has a small airstrip which connect the islands to Panama City. Consequently, it is comparatively busy with occasional disturbances in the main anchorage from aeroplanes landing and departing at the nearby runway. Ashore there are several small shops but most only sell tourist items. For immigration procedures, find the administrative building near to the public jetty.\nPanama Canal Transit Information World Cruising Club employ the services of a transit agent in order to expedite the fleet’s transit of the Canal. Recreational yachts are not the most important user of the canal and as a result, the procedures for the transit of pleasure craft are subject to changes. The following text outlines what to expect when transiting the Canal, based on the procedures in place at the time of writing.\nPaperwork Prior to the start of the transit, World Cruising Club will collect details on each yacht (boat details, scantling lists, crew details, scans of passports and boat’s papers, etc.) and declarations from each skipper that they understand and will comply with the terms and conditions of the transit. As an example, each skipper must declare that their vessel is capable of motoring at a boat speed in excess of 6 knots. This paperwork will be passed onto the transit agent and must therefore be provided in good time in order not to cause any delays.\nPreparation Since 2024 inspections and admeasurements are done virtually through ASEM (Small Craft Service Management). The process will be managed by the World ARC agent and Rally Control, and includes the upload of various photos of the boat to demonstrate main measurements and projecting deck equipment, and dimensions.\nThe LOA is the total length of the vessel to determine space requirements within the locks. This should include davits, stem head fittings, bowsprits and other protuberances (i.e. transom generators etc.). This measurement will be used to determine the price bracket charged for transit. Yachts close to the 65’plus bracket will require a pilot rather than an advisor for the transit should consider removing items not required for the transit to ensure they fall into the lower bracket if at all possible. Measurers’ decisions are final and will not be influenced by evidence of other LOA documentation or ratings measurements.\nIn-person admeasures might be required whilst in Shelter Bay if photos or measurements are questionable.\nPilot / Advisor Yachts under 65’ will generally have an advisor onboard in place of a pilot. They are generally Panama Canal employees or even pilots, who are volunteering to act as advisors during their overtime. It should be remembered that advisors are just advisors and the skipper should remain cautious. There are many different advisors (around 300) and the service and manner of each differs greatly – after all, they are working overtime and not sat on a comfy bridge! Most crews find that treating them as an ‘Examiner on Board’ keeps them in an agreeable mood. They will expect to be fed whilst onboard.\naboard. San Blas white sand beaches Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 34 / 03. Local Information Line Handlers Each yacht is required to have 4 line handlers These are crew members (aged over 16 years old) in addition to the skipper and the advisor/pilot. The line handlers need to be aware at all times. It is possible to hire line handlers, but experienced yacht crew would always be preferable. As with any manoeuvres, line handlers need to be well briefed on what commands will be used to ensure that the boat is firmly held (but not suspended).\nLines and Fenders Mooring lines and fenders will be hired by World Cruising Club for the yachts. These lines are approximately 40mm diameter and 40m long. One end should have a 1m diameter loop on it for placing over the lock bollards. The line handlers will then control the lines as the yacht rises and falls in the locks. Lines are returned at the end of the transit.\nFenders are approx. 80cm-1m in diameter and are to be used on both sides of the nest (raft).\nLocks The lock chambers are huge and measure 300x33m. This means that the flow rates into and out of the locks are quite high. The water moves through 100 holes in the floor of each chamber. This normally creates a large but equal disturbance which is not really a problem if you are in a chamber with other rafts of yachts. However, if a raft is chambered with a larger (cargo) vessel and generally this will be to her stern, the under-floor currents are displaced by her hull and can create a current off their stern. Just be ready with the throttle and make sure line handlers are alert. Similarly, anticipate that when the gates open the larger vessel will engage forward gear and wash will kick astern. When descending towards the Pacific, in Miraflores locks, be aware of a potentially dangerous current which sets yachts hard towards the final gate when manoeuvring within the last chamber.\nSchedule A typical transit will begin with a departure from Shelter Bay Marina early in the morning for the daily transits and in the late afternoon for the overnight transits. The advisor will board the yacht, in the “flats” area in the vicinity of Shelter Bay Marina, or will meet the yacht in the marina, before travelling with the yacht up the locks to Gatun Lake. On exiting the last ‘up’ lock (potentially as late as midnight), yachts on the overnight passage will tie up to a mooring buoy, in the lake, and a Pilot Vessel will pick up the advisor. The advisor will re-board at about 07:00 for a dawn journey through the Panama Canal before starting the “down” locks at Pedro Miguel and Miraflores. Expect to exit the last lock at about lunchtime, from where it is about an hour to the end of the Amador Causeway.\nDaily passages don’t stop until arriving on the Pacific side usually late in the evening.\nThe preferred schedule for the World ARC fleet is for boats to be divided into nests (rafts) of three. Nests tie up and separate on the instructions of advisors in the mouth of the locks.\nCrews should expect the possibility of being asked to join boats on an earlier transit to help with their lines, and then travel back on land to make their own transit. To complete the favour, crews who have made the transits might be asked to return to Shelter Bay to assist others. This will allow the fleet to transit the Canal without the need to employ extra line handlers.\nTeam Kincsem, Panama Canal Transit Panama Canal Transit Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 35 © World Cruising Club 2026 Team Aurelia T, Panama Canal transit The Bridge of the Americas Large ship in the Panama Canal Panama Canal transit Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 36 / 03. Local Information There are three possible mooring options on the Pacific side of the Canal. Flamenco (A) and La Playita (B) Marinas may have space for a limited time. The marinas are situated between the islands of Perico and Flamenco, which are attached to the mainland by a thin causeway. Ashore on the mainland are the districts of Balboa and Amador and, slightly further afield, the country’s capital, Panama City. Both Marinas are well situated for exploring Panama City and beyond.\nIf there is no space available, yachts can anchor in the shelter of the Amador causeway (C). World ARC uses La Playita Marina (B). A. Flamenco Marina Facilities Flamenco Marina opened in 2004 on the Pacific approaches to the Panama Canal. There is a cruisers’ net on VHF Ch. 74 every morning at 08:00, operated from the anchorage near Flamenco Marina. Some useful information is broadcasted. Docking At Flamenco Marina, berthing is alongside one of 165 floating pontoons. B. La Playita Marina La Playita Marina lies alongside the Pacific approaches to the Canal, on the opposite side of the Islands to the larger Flamenco Marina. Docking Two pontoons, each with 40 slips, most of which are privately owned. Depths on the second pontoon should be monitored carefully – particularly during big spring tides when low water can be considerably less than chart datum. Panama City A. Flamenco Marina Isla Flamenco Calzada de Amador Apdo 6464, Zone 5, Panama Location:\t8°54’53N 79°31’9W VHF: Ch.10 T: +507 314 1980 / 1353 Email: service@fuerteamador.com Website: fuerteamador.com Water: Potable Power 110V/220V 60Hz B. La Playita Marina Isla Naos, Calzada de Amador, Panama Location:\t8°54’44N 79°31’34W VHF: Ch.9 T: +507 6000 5127 Email: laplayita@siwireless.com Water: Potable Power 110V/220V 60Hz C. Anchorage Location: 8°54’36N, 79°31’30”W C A B Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 37 © World Cruising Club 2026 La Playita Marina is not normally open to visiting yachts, but World ARC has a good relationship with this small marina and occasionally some space is made available for the fleet. With multiple World ARC transit groups, there is a strong possibility that early arrivals will be required to move on after a couple of days to make room for later arrivals. Facilities Shore access to pontoons is controlled by electronically controlled gates. A key card is required which must be collected at check-in. A deposit may be required. There is a small toilet and shower block. The dinghy dock can be used for vessels at anchor after payment of a fee for World ARC yachts.\nA small laundry (washers and dryers) is available. Cost US$2 per load. Machines only take quarters ($0.25). A minimarket can be found next to the marina office with cold drinks, snacks and some other goodies.\nElectricity & Water There is NO 16AMP POWER AVAILABLE. Slips are mainly 30A, some 50A. Note adapters from 30A to 16A have proved impossible to source in the past. The Dockmaster may have a few converters. 30A = 110V, 50A = 220V both supplies are at 60Hz. The marina has limited electrical outlets that can be obtained from the marina office. A deposit will be required. Water is potable.\nFuel Diesel is available in the alongside slip, but times must be booked in advance with the office. VISA and Mastercard accepted, but not AMEX. It is not advisable to use the fuel dock when approaching low tide. Boats with larger draft may benefit from calling Flamenco Marina in advance to organise refuelling at their deeper fuel dock. Sanitation Charge In either Marina, yachts may be visited by the local health department and asked to pay a one-off sanitation charge of US$15-$30.\nVisiting Taboga Island The Taboga ferry departs from La Playita marina every day (45 minutes trip). Note that at weekends you will need to start getting in line nearly an hour in advance! Ferries depart Panama Mon-Fri at 08:30 and return from Taboga at 16:30. On weekends and holidays, ferries depart Panama at 08:30, 10:30 and 1630 and return from Taboga at 09:00, 15:00 and 17:00.\nA to Z of Boat Services Boatyard & Haulout Flamenco Marina flamencomarina.com Large yard for onshore minor repairs and cleaning work. All repair work must be carried out by authorised external service companies. Good 24hr security. Liveaboard ashore is not permitted. 150 ton travelift (max 100ft LOA/26ft beam). Vista Mar Marina vistamarmarina.com 20NM from La Playita- hydraulic trailer for catamarans and yachts up to 95 tons. Contact Jaqueline +507 6611 8528.\nAbysa Yachting abysa.com.pa Full service marine company located in Amador Boatyard next to Flamenco Marina.\nChandleries Abernathy’s abernathypanama.com Several locations in Panama City, including Amador Causeway, opposite La Playita Marina Office. Marine supplies, fishing supplies, clothing, rope, Jabsco pumps. Islamorada islamorada.com Charts, SSB and VHF equipment, almanacs. 5 min taxi from La Playita. Representatives of Volvo Penta, B&G, SIMRAD, Lewmar, and others. Office opposite La Playita Marina.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 38 / 03.
