There is also an excellent selection of restaurants and bars in the Old Town.\nShopping & Supermarkets Shops in the shopping plaza sell a limited range of goods, however there are three large shopping malls 15 mins away by taxi.\nSupercentro El Dorado Shopping Mall with Super 99 and El Rey supermarkets. Albrook Mall albrookmall.com Wide variety of stores including electronics, clothes, gifts, fresh fruit and veg and a very good supermarket with good meat counter.\nMulti Plaza multiplaza.com/panama Premium brand shopping mall with large Riba Smith supermarket.\nTelephone SIM cards are available to purchase in most minimarkets and Albrook Mall has a number of mobile stores.\nDoctors / Hospitals Emergency ambulance: 911 There are many very good private medical facilities in Panama City.\nSanta Fe Hospital hospitalsantafepanama.com +507 2274733 Centro Medico Patilla hospitalpaitilla.com 24hr emergency room. +507 2658888 Team Celeste, Panama Canal transit 2026 Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 40 / 03. Local Information Travel Information Airports Nearest International airport is Tocumen Airport, Panama City and is around 30-45 minutes taxi drive away. With flights to the Caribbean, North and South America and Europe. Direct flights to/from Europe are available with KLM and Iberia. Tocumen Airport tocumenpanama.aero Buses mibus.com.pa Panama has one of the most accessible and most efficient bus systems in the Americas. A bus makes the Isla Flamenco circle once an hour. Beyond the Isla, there are many buses serving Panama City.\nBicycle Rental Moses Bike Rental mosesbikerentals.com The Causeway, Amador. Rent bikes, e-bike and e-scooters. Car Rental Alamo alamo.com +507 2365777 Hertz hertz.com +507 2600000 Taxis There are cabs in the Flamenco Island area frequently and the ride for two into Amador is US$3, Panama City around US$10. The journey to Tocumen Airport is typically US$50, and the ride back to Colón (after a Canal transit) is US$120. Uber Also available.\nMy Notes Team Alpha Centauri arrive in Panama City Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 41 © World Cruising Club 2026 Las Perlas Islands A. Isla del Rey B. San Jose C. Galera D. Contadora Island Location: 08°37’20”N 79° 02’08”W The Archipelago de Las Perlas is made up of more than 220 islands and islets, only 90 of which are named and even fewer are inhabited. These islands first gained notoriety in 1513 in the days of Vasco Nuñez de Balboa’s discovery of the Pacific Ocean. It is said that he encountered a group of Indians, resplendent in pearls, paddling boats who told him of the islands and their rich pearl beds. Although he never actually went to the islands, he named them “Islas de Las Perlas” (Pearl Islands).\nEntry Formalities The islands are administered by the Republic of Panama. If one intends to stop at Las Perlas after clearing out from mainland Panama, this should be mentioned on the clearance papers. No immigration is available in Las Perlas.\nPilotage Information Charts 1929 - Gulf of Panama.\n2258 - Bahia Buenaventura to Panama.\nPilots Panama Cruising Guide (Bauhaus).\nA word of caution: Tides in the Pacific can be as large as 5 metres. Take care when finding an anchorage and when landing a dinghy ashore. Contadora Island, Las Perlas Islands to visit There are plenty of anchorages to choose from, mostly deserted. There are navigational challenges to stopping in the Las Perlas Islands. However, once these are overcome, you will hopefully enjoy the peace and tranquillity of the islands. With Contadora, the three most notable islands are: Isla del Rey: The largest island is Isla del Rey (“Island of the King”). Isla del Rey has several towns, most notably San Miguel. It offers many interesting inland areas to explore. San Jose: San Jose is the second largest island, surrounded by over 35 white, black or gold beaches and coves. Lush green vegetation covers the whole island over gently sloping hills which peak, at the north of the island, to 440 feet. The coast line is very irregular, with many anchorages, a favourite is Bodega Bay, on the west coast. Galera The lonely island of Galera is beautiful beyond description, with its heavy facade of tall coconut palm trees and a white sandy beach. Be aware that there are very strong currents, shallows and reefs surrounding the island and navigation can be especially tricky.\nA B D C Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 42 / 03. Local Information Contadora Island isladecontadora.com Contadora Island is the most developed island. It is home to a small resort, with a small airport, hotels, restaurants, a couple of shops and a service station. Be careful when choosing your anchorage, as here is the only legal naturist beach in South America! Contadora is about 750 acres, is crossed by several roads, and can be walked around in an hour and half.\nContadora Location: 08°37’20”N 79° 02’08”W Mooring The prevailing NE winds make the south side of the island more sheltered than the north. There is a large anchorage to the west of the airstrip approach (Playa Cacique). There are alternative anchorages on the south side to the east of the airstrip (Playa Suecas) and also to the east and north of the island as well (Playa Larga and Playa Galleon). A to Z of Boat Services No real yacht facilities or services available on the Islands.\nFuel Approximately US$5 per gallon for diesel, US$6.40 per gallon for gas oil.\nGas (Cooking) Propane bottles are available at the Contadora Supermarket but no refills or top ups are available.\nA to Z of What’s On Shore Accommodation Villa Mar Y Oro Hotel facebook.com/maryorohotel maryororeservation@gmail.com The Point Hotel hotelthepoint.com reservations@hotelthepoint.com Hotel Gerald Gerald@cableonda.net +507 65881046 Diving Coral Dreams/Diving Contadora scubalasperlas.com Run by Roxana and Guillermo who offer diving and snorkeling tours.\nLaundry Laundry service is available via the Mar Y Oro Hotel Money Arrive with sufficient US dollars for your stay as there is no bank or ATM. Hotels accept Visa and Mastercard but there is an additional surcharge.\nRestaurants Mar Y Oro Hotel +507 202 7426 Seafood and international cuisine. Also serves breakfast. Sal y Pimienta +507 6615 1451 Set menu and pizzas. Rincon Hot Stone Near Police station.\nDoctors / Hospitals Emergency ambulance: 911 Contadora Island offers basic healthcare services to its residents and visitors. The island has a small health clinic that provides primary care services, including routine check-ups, treatment of minor illnesses and injuries, and emergency medical care Health Centre open Mon-Fri from 07:00-15:00.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 43 © World Cruising Club 2026 Blandy +507 2504037 Serves basic meals including breakfast. Dinners from 17:00-19:00. A La Fonda Clarita +507 6518 5396 Serves breakfast (06:30-08:30), Lunch (1145-1330 and dinner (18:00-20:00) Restaurant Gerald +507 250 4159 Seafood German food, pizzas and pastas. Serves breakfast. Rubbish Small quantities of rubbish from Playa Cacique may be taken ashore at the Mar Y Oro Hotel.\nShopping Contadora Supermarket The biggest of the mini markets. Open 08:00-19:00, closed for lunch between 13:00-14:00. Blandy With some fresh vegetables and frozen meat. Open 07:00-23:00, with fresh grocery resupply from Tuesday- Thursday. Pacific Island Shopette Open 07:00-22:00.\nTourist Information The Contadora Welcome Centre isladecontadora.com Offers car, bike and kayak rentals, and can assist with laundry services and hotel/travel bookings. WiFi also available.\nTravel Information Car Rental Contadora is a small island, and although most of the roads are paved, there are few vehichles. People get around by walking, by bicycle (US$15/day) or more popularly, by ATV(US$75/day) or golf cart (US$60/day)/ The Welcome Centre can assist with car rentals and taxis. Open 08:00-19:00. isladecontadora.com Ferries Sea Las Perlas ferrypearlislands.com/en/ Daily ferries to and from Contadora and Panama.\nFlights Flights from Panama City https://flytrip.com.pa/ Flights to Contadora (OTD) fromMarcos A. Gelabert International Airport.\nMy Notes Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 44 / 03. Local Information Galapagos Islands Local Information Dialling Code: +593 5 Time zone: UTC -6 Currency: US$ Emergency: 911 Language: Spanish Useful Websites: ecuador.travel galapagos.org Preparing for Galapagos The Galapagos Islands are a special stopover on the World ARC schedule. Ever since the work of Charles Darwin, the islands have been noted for their contribution to the understanding of evolution, and in the present day, there are many examples of flora, fauna and sea life to be appreciated.\nThere are many protection measures in place to try to reduce human impact on the natural habitat which can make it difficult to work out the best way to appreciate the islands. Please see the World ARC Galapagos guide on the Members Area which contains all the mandatory requirements as well as tips, advice and details of tours and excursions to explore the islands. Entry Formalities The Galapagos Islands are one of the most fascinating parts of the World but they are also among the most regulated. The islands are governed from mainland Ecuador and rules change on a regular basis. We employ a local agent to assist with the formalities and permits for the World ARC fleet. World Cruising does its best to prepare you for the formalities be prepared to expect last minute changes.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 45 © World Cruising Club 2026 Pilotage Information Charts RCEC22000 Galapagos Islands with Plans Pilots Landfalls of Paradise (Hinz) Pacific Crossing Guide (RCC) South Pacific Handbook (Stanley) Cruising the South Pacific (Austin) San Cristobal Puerto Baquerizo Location:\t00°53’55”S 89°36’45”W Deliveries As courier delays and customs hold-ups regularly occur in mainland Ecuador, it is strongly advised that no items are shipped for collection or receipt during the World ARC stopover in Galapagos.\nCrew and Boat Documentation The process of obtaining permits begins well in advance of your arrival which is why we ask for crew details, passport scans and copies of boat documentation (i.e. ship’s papers and GRT) several months in advance of the stopover. Please be aware that last minute changes to crew lists, or late provision of documents can incur additional charges from the authorities which will be passed on at cost. Preparing your boat Regulations to prevent bio-contamination are strictly enforced. Yachts arriving with any signs of fouling are likely to be denied entry and required to depart at least 60NM offshore to clean hulls and fittings before attempting re-entry. A re-inspection fee of US$150 will be charged. The risk of this can be reduced by thorough preparation. We recommend starting World ARC with fresh antifouling, thorough hull cleaning before leaving Panama, employing divers in Panama City and giving the hull a quick final ‘once over’ at sea if/when conditions permit before arrival. Keep receipts as evidence of when anti-fouling and cleaning has been last carried out. Flying to and from the Galapagos When flying to the Galapagos Islands you must transit through mainland Ecuador to either Quito or Guayaquil and then take a connecting flight (see specific travel information for each island). You may need to arrange overnight accommodation.\nCrew joining in the Galapagos Islands who have a one-way inbound ticket must email Rally Control a copy of their airline ticket and their booking code. An approval from the GNP (Galapagos National Park) will be required before onward travels. Crew departing by plane or friends and family visiting the Galapagos on a two-way ticket do not require this documentation. Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 46 / 03. Local Information General Puerto Baquerizo on San Cristobal Island will be the first scheduled stop for World ARC in the Galapagos. It is a small, compact, safe town with all basic necessities just a walk from the dock. You should be able to orientate yourself quite quickly with what it has to offer. Note: drones are not allowed to be flown anywhere over towns in Galapagos.\nMooring The fleet will anchor in Wreck Bay (Bahia de Naufragio) at Puerto Baquerizo Moreno. Keep clear of the unmarked channel. Yachts may be required to lay a stern anchor. Stay to the NE of the anchorage to keep clear of the service vessel.\nWater It is possible to get fresh water delivered to a boat at in 4 gallon containers. Enquire through Rally Control. A to Z of Boat Services San Cristobal has very limited facilities and anything, but the smallest repair would be difficult to carry out here. Puerto Ayora on Santa Cruz Island has marine repair specialists. Chandlery There is a selection of small hardware stores where it’s possible to get general items. The shops, known as ‘Ferreteria’ stock a reasonable range of fixtures, fittings, ropes, hoses, tools etc. Some also sell fishing equipment. The closest Ferreteria is 50m from the dock.\nElectrical There is an electrician (Miguel) who has a small workshop in the middle of the town (in the coffee field). He is willing, but has limited equipment and parts.\nMetal Work Mecanica Industrial “Ebly Chavez” +593 5 998924600 Metal workshop located at the edge of town who can fabricate good replica metal work for damaged pins etc. (No road name - located at 0°54’32.8”S 89°36’26.3”W) Fuel Enquire through Rally Control - visitors wishing to purchase fuel in the Galapagos must do so through an agent as a permit is required. International prices change every month. Fuel is available in San Cristobal; however, only in small quantities. The maximum amount of fuel in Puerto Baquerizo is 100 US Gallons due to limited supplies on the island, needed by the locals. Larger quantities of fuel are available in Puerto Ayora. More information regarding fuel in the Galapagos will be communicated at the preceding Skippers Briefing.\nGas (Cooking) Not available on San Cristobal.\nA to Z of What’s on Shore Accommodation Miconia Hotel and Restaurant hotelmiconia.com Galapagos Sunset Hotel galapagossunset.com.ec Golden Bay Hotel & Spa goldenbay.com.ec/wp/ Various B&B style lodgings available around the town, varying in quality. The Tourist Information office has more details.\nIce Available in local food shops or bars, including the Miconia Hotel and Lavanderia Rosita.\nInternet The majority of restaurants and cafes have WiFi. Laundry Expect to pay around US$2/kg, for wash and dry service. Lavanderia Mocking Bird Av. Espanola Limpio y Seco Av. Alsacia Northia Rosita Av. Espanola Laundry Av. Alsacio Northa Doctors / Hospitals Emergency ambulance: 911 Hospital Oskar Jandal +593 525 20 332 salud.gob.ec/hospital-basico-oskar-jandal Red Cross +593 52520125 Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 47 © World Cruising Club 2026 Money Banco del Pacifico bancodelpacifico.com A few ATMs in town. All dispensing USD. One ATM located opposite Café UMAMI. Pharmacy Five pharmacies within the town, all fairly well stocked. Duty system to provide 24hr availability. Restaurants UMAMI Café and Restaurant +593 99 958 5633 Serving breakfast, lunch and dinner, located on the beach front just past the Post Office.\nPost Office postofficegalapagos.com The old post office has been converted into a restaurant and bar serving food, beer and cocktails, located on the beachfront to the right of dock.\nGiuseppe Restaurant facebook.com/GiuseppesRest Italian restaurant serving fresh pizzas and pasta dishes located on the beach front towards the local information centre.\nMiconia Restaurant hotelmiconia.com On the beach front, a 50m walk from the dock. Overlooks the anchorage. This venue has become popular for both drinks and dinner.\nMidori Sushi Bar/Restaurant midorigalapagos.com Located on Av. Charles Darwin. Open from 12:00.\nMockingbird Restaurant Serves mostly fish. Open 10:00-23:00.\nRubbish Please do not bring your rubbish ashore independently. Rally Control will make special arrangements and a boat will collect your rubbish bags on designated days. US$3 per bag to be paid to the boatman. Spanish for rubbish is ‘basura’. All bags need to be identified as recyclable, non-recyclable or organic waste. Additionally, there is a rubbish boat that collects each morning in Puerto Ayora.\nSports Facilities Cycle, surf & SUP hire available in numerous outlets along the main street and ajoing roads.\nShopping Markets for fresh produce and a few supermarkets available. Most small stores shut for siesta in the afternoon. Generally, avoid shopping on a Sunday.\nBakery Kachi Tanta Artisan bakery and pastry shop. Sourdough. A sustainable project focused on empowering women. Avenida 12 de Febrero with Alsacio Northia. Opens daily 06:00-20:00 Sabor Cuencano Bakery and pastry shop. Alsacio Northia and Federico Garcia. Mon-Fri 06:00-22:00; Sat-Sun 06:00-11:30 Markets Mercado Municipal Good fruit, vegetable, fish and meat market located on the corner of Avenida 12 de Febrero and Juan Jose Flores. Open Mon-Fri 06:00-1800. Sat-Sun until 12:00.\nSupermarkets Several small minimarts available across town.\nSupermercado Galamaxi Located on Flores and Av. Quito.\nFoods & Healthy Market Located on Avenida Alsacio Northia. Other small minimarts available across town.\nToilets The only public toilet available is by the pier, but no showers.\nTourist Information The municipal Tourist Information is located a couple of blocks back from the front and near the church at the corner of Av. Charles Darwin and 12 de Febrero. (opposite Café UMAMI).\nBicycle Rental Chalo Tours chalotours.org Ave Espanola (next to police station) hire bikes. Cañon Point Adventure Shop @canonpointgalapagos Bike hire and bike tours. Office close to the main pier.\nExpect to pay around US$35 per day including transport to the highlands.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 48 / 03. Local Information Floreana Puerto Velazco Ibarra Location:\t1°16’50.68”S 90°29’28 .97”W General Floreana opened to World ARC Rally yachts for the first time in 2026. Rally participants can now include a visit to this unique island in their free sailing time, whilst in the Galapagos.\nThe 90NM transit from San Cristobal to Isabela, can be reduced to 70NM with a stopover in Floreana, in the south of the Galapagos archipelago. Leaving San Cristobal early in the morning, a mid-afternoon arrival in Floreana is achievable. Floreana is one of four inhabited islands in the Galapagos. Shaped by volcanic activity and erosion, this small island with 102 inhabitants is also home to several endangered species, such as the Galapagos storm petrel. In 2026 the Floreana giant tortoise was re-introduced for the first time in over 180 years. Turtles, marine iguanas and sea lions are more plentiful. They can often be seen from the beaches and anchorage. Don’t Miss Las Tijeretas Bay Just a 30-minute walk from the village you can swim and snorkel with sea lions and marine iguanas. The route out of the village passes Playa Mann beach and the Interpretation Centre, as well as the well-marked and paved trails that wind around the scrub-covered Cerro de las Tijeretas. There are paths leading to panoramic viewpoints, as well as to Playa Punta Carola, where there’s great surfing. Travel Information Airports Airport Isla San Cristobal (SCY) Around 2km from the town centre.\nAirlines Airlines offering flights to the mainland. Avianca avianca.com LATAM https://www.latamairlines.com/gb/en Inter-island carriers Emetebe emetebe.com.ec Esav Airlines esavairlines.com Daily flights connecting San Cristóbal, Isabela, and Baltra – Santa Cruz Ferries Daily ferry service from San Cristobal to Puerto Ayora on Santa Cruz, departing at 07:00 and 15:00, then returning at 15:00 and 07:00. It takes 2hr– 2.5hrs and costs approximately US$35 (2026). Book a few days in advance.\nTaxis Taxis are double-cab 4x4 pick-ups. Just hail one down in the street. You can also hire the driver on an hourly basis to conduct an island tour. Cost approx. US$2 per stop.\nWater Taxis Taxi Acuatico VHF Ch. 14. US$1 per person during daylight, US$2 after 18:00.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 49 © World Cruising Club 2026 What’s On Shore?\nFloreana has very few facilities and services are scarce. There are no ATMs and cards are not accepted. There are two small stores in Puerto Velasco Ibarra. Dining can be found at Hotel Wittmer on the sea front, the Lecolarpus (no menu), and Canchalagua (pizzaria).\nWhere to stay?\nHotel Wittmer +593 99 024 5650 In front of the anchorage. Owned by the daughter of the original settlers that came in the 1930’s. Visit the museum and perhaps buy the book on which the film ‘Eden’ was based.\nFloreana Lava Lodge A 10-room, eco-friendly lodge constructed from mainland pine, situated on the black lava sands near the anchorage. Known for being quiet and offering a relaxing, rustic stay.\nWater, Fuel, Gas and Rubbish Water, fuel, gas and waste collection are not available in Floreana.\nPharmacy No pharmacy. There is a small dispensary here, but we advise contacting Rally Control for more specific information on where best to find help.\nTravelling Around There is a local bus, open-sided wooden ‘chiva’, that operates to transport visitors from the pier and black beach to the highlands for tours. This, along with pickup trucks, is the primary mode of transportation for accessing inland sites like the pirate caves and Asilo de La Paz.\nLanchas (speedboats) to and from Floreana Island operate primarily between Puerto Ayora (Santa Cruz) and Puerto Velasco Ibarra (Floreana), taking approximately 2 hours. Scheduled public ferries generally depart Santa Cruz at 08:00 and return from Floreana around 15:00, costing around $40-$45 per person. There are many tales of pirates using the island as a hideout in the 16th and 17th centuries and whalers often stopped at the island to provision. It was a British whaling ship that set up a post office using an empty barrel in 1793 to send mail home with returning ships – a service that could take two years! This can still be seen and used today. More recently, Floreana is known for the true events that happened on the island in the late 20s and 30s and inspired the movie ‘Eden’. Well worth watching before visiting the island (please note this movie is not suitable for children).\nFloreana, Galapagos Entry Formalities Entry formalities for Floreana are completed on behalf of the skipper by the World ARC agent, as soon as free sailing plans are submitted. There is a small additional charge (US$30 February 2026) for entry and exit clearances.\nMooring The anchorage has plenty of space and is exposed to the west. It can occasionally experience swell from the west and northwest. Drop anchor in front of Hotel Wittmer. The bottom is sand and good holding. Don’t attempt to anchor close to shore as the bottom is volcanic rock. Tenders may be used to drop people off at the concrete dock but cannot be tied up, as this space is reserved for local boats. Dinghies are not allowed to go to beach areas. A water taxi operates during the daytime and can be called on VHF Ch.14.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 50 / 03. Local Information General Puerto Villamil can best be described as the “jewel of the Galapagos harbours”. The harbour is protected by a southern string of low islands and reefs and is therefore normally a protected, calm anchorage. Villamil is a small but bustling place. The locals are very friendly and welcoming to tourists. On previous visits there has been a sense of excitement and pride in the town upon the arrival of yachts from the fleet. There is limited infrastructure, which only complements an excellent sense of adventure.\nIsabela Puerto Villamil Location:\t00°57’55”S, 90°57’45”W Don’t Miss ● Playa Negra – a public beach with black volcanic sand a short walk away. Great for snorkelling, turtles and sea lions. ● La Loberia beach - reached by a short walk along the shoreline. Good for seeing large marine iguanas, and sea lions. ● Take a trip to Post Office Bay and the mail barrel. The walk is two hours one way and return to town by boat. ● Take a tour to visit the Highlands and the pineapple plantation, Asilo de Paz, Pirate Caves and Tortoise Corrals, where the first settlers used to live.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 51 © World Cruising Club 2026 Formalities Formalities to be briefed by the World ARC Agent. If visiting Isabela it must be included in your sail plan and passed to Rally Control before leaving San Cristobal. On arrival at the pier you may be asked to pay a one-off US$10 visitors landing fee – you will be issued a receipt for future visits ashore.\nMooring The harbour is protected by a southern string of low islands and reefs. While there is a small boat entry through these islands, yachts will need to round to the south and west of the group, and enter through a buoyed channel. Entry is not easy as it is encumbered with reefs, so make sure you arrive with good light and keep a good lookout for reefs in the approaches to the anchorage.\nThe anchorage area is bounded by low volcanic islets, is quite calm and is a lot smaller than it looks as you enter, because it is mostly filled with submerged reefs and shoals. Reefs are above water at low tide.\nThe water in this protected area is quite shallow (2 to 4 metres). There are large coral slabs on the bottom so it is easy to foul the anchor, but the anchorage is well protected from the swell. Be aware of small lumps and bumps that rise up quickly from the bottom. The anchorage is large enough for around 15 yachts, and becomes shallower the further in you go.\nWater Cruisers have bought locally produced agua desalinada in 5 gallon jugs from Francisco M: +593 86214512. US$2 per 5 gallons.\nDinghy Dock The small dinghy dock is to the east of the town (called the embarcadero). Unlike other anchorages you are able to leave your dinghy, however, it must only be used to get to and from your boat. Do not snorkel from your dinghy or go to surrounding beaches. Sea lions remain a threat to your dinghy, but the threat is smaller here than on other islands. It is advisable to use a water taxi for your visit ashore before you consider your dinghy. A water-taxi can be called on Ch. 14 and charges US$2 per person until 21:00. Occasionally, the water taxi may run after 21:00, and will charge more - approx. US$5 per person.\nTo reach town from the embarcadero, follow the road, and stay left when the road branches. Town is about a 15-20 minute walk away.\nA to Z of Boat Services Chandleries Two hardware stores in town, and most supermarkets also have a small hardware section. Ferreteria Ana Karolina Calle Escalecia, next to Isabela Dive Center has quite a bit of stainless fasteners, tools, gasket material, etc. Ferreteria Las Palmas Via Comoran and El Gaviotin.\nFuel Plan to receive fuel in Puerto Baquerizo or Puerto Ayora.\nMechanical Taller Peter +593 52529436/+593 86925177 Located on Via Municipal, one block west of the blue-roofed municipal equipment repair shop. Metalwork and Welding Taller Peter (See Mechanical) Aluminium, stainless, monel, steel, and bronze welding. Woodwork David Playito apolinario_7@hotmail.com Isabela Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 52 / 03. Local Information A to Z of What’s On Shore Accommodation Several hotels and hostels available on the beach front and in town.\nHotel Albermarle hotelalbemarle.com La Casa de Marita Boutique Hotel lacasademarita.com Drake Inn drakeinngalapagos.com The Wooden House Lodge thewoodenhouselodge.com Internet Most restaurants and cafes along the main road have Wi-Fi available. Laundry Concha de Perla Lavanderia Short walk inland from the embarcadero. US$1.50/ kg, 24hr return.\nLavanderia Jordan US$2/kg. Wash, dry, fold.\nMoney It is recommended that you arrive with enough currency. The most accessible ATM can be found in the town square on the corner furthest away from the embarcadero, there are normal international withdrawal charges but be advised if it does run out of funds, it will be a few days before it is refilled. Many shops and restaurants will only be able to give change for small denominations of cash, a $20 can often pose a challenge. Having a supply of $1 bills can come in handy. Restaurants El Cafetal Cafe Cultural @elcafetal_galapagos (Instagram) Nice quiet relaxing place for a drink.\nLa Choza, Cesars or El Encanto de la Pepe. Serve local food all day. On main square.\nPizzeria Los Delfines On main square. Pizzas around $20, very good internet café with a pool table.\nBar de Beto hostalgalapagos.com/bardebeto/ The second to last building on the beach as you walk west out of town. A relaxed atmosphere and happy hour in the evening. The Beach House Dine with your toes in the sand at this lovely establishment on the beachfront.\nGracias Madre Great little Mexican restaurant just off the main square.\nEl Faro 100m north of the main square on via Las Fragatas.\nPink Iguana iguanaville.com/pink-iguana-bar The last establishment on the beach as you walk out of town (away from the dinghy dock). Booby Trap Restaurant & Bar Opposite Casa Rosada. Rubbish There is a water taxi which collects rubbish from the boats. Expect to pay US$5 per bag of waste. Shopping Bakery: Panaderia Frigata Open Mon-Sat 06:00-09:00 and 16:00-19:00.\nButcher: Air-conditioned butcher shop run by Mercedes (on the right by the bus stop just past the triangle and grocery store). Beef, chicken etc. available. Supermarkets: Provisioning on Isabela is a bit hit and miss with a number of minimarkets doted around the town. Supply boats are scheduled regularly every 15 days or so.\nVolcano Rock Fresh local fruits, vegetables, meats, fish, and ready-made meals - will deliver to your boat. On Av. 16 de marzo y Las Fragatas.\nDoctors / Hospitals Emergency ambulance: 911 There is only one health facility (Centro de Salud) located on the streets 16 de Marzo and Malecón / Open 0730 to 1700. +593 52529181 Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 53 © World Cruising Club 2026 Travel Information Airport José de Villamil Airport (IBB) Inter-island flights only Airlines Inter-island carriers Emetebe emetebe.com.ec ESAV Airlines esavairlines.com Daily flights connecting San Cristóbal, Isabela, and Baltra – Santa Cruz Taxis Relatively inexpensive. Around US$2 per person for a trip to town from the dinghy dock.\nBicycle Rentals Galápagos Bike & Surf galapagosbikeandsurf.com Bike hire as well as bodyboards, surfboard and paddle board hire. Also do tours and surf lessons.\nBICI BANG bicibang.com Regular and electric mountain bike hire, prices from US$7 & US$13 respectively.\nFerry Passenger speed boats operate daily between Isabela and Santa Cruz. The journey takes 2-3hrs and costs US$30. Boats depart the embarcadero at 06:00 or 15:00. The return journey from Santa Cruz departs at 14:00. The ride can be wet and uncomfortable in certain sea conditions but is useful for managing crew changes. To book, visit the different tour operators in town.\nDon’t Miss Plan your tours in advance of arriving on the Island. Most tours depart early and will need to be organised by midday on the day before. Concha y Perla A raised walkway through ancient mangroves to a tranquil pool with a dock - great place for snorkling and a picnic.\nLas Tintoreas A trail around the reef and islets surrounding the anchorage. See penguins, turtles, marine iguanas, white tipped reef sharks, sea lions, and blue footed boobies. Centro de Crianza Trail and raised walkways to tortoise rescue and breeding centre. On route you’ll also see flamingos. Entry to the tortoise rescue centre is US$10 but the walk is free.\nMuro de Lágrimas The ‘wall of tears’ is 6 km along the beach road and was built after WWII by inmates of a penal colony. From there, go to the Mirador (overlook) and then walk along the trails. Lava tubes, Playa del Amor (covered in marine iguanas), lagoons with pink flamingos (6pm when they return from feeding). You can arrange a taxi for US$12 to take you to the Wall, wait, take you to the Mirador, and then take you to the trails and then walk back to town. Biking is also a nice option.\nCerro Negro and Volcan Chico Volcanoes All day trip by truck and hiking. Even if it is cloudy and rainy in the anchorage, it will more than likely be clear at the volcanoes (or vice versa). Check conditions before you go to avoid disappointment. Cabo Rosa/Los Tuneles A 25 mile panga ride to Cabo Rosa, with a brief stop at Roca Union. Cabo Rosa (or Los Tuneles) is an unbelievable lava landscape with a series of narrow channels and arches with cactus growing on them. The collapsed lava has created many tunnels and bridges. Snorkelling possible nearby too. This is a popular to so be sure to book early.\nTortuga Island Boat trip to Tortuga Island to see its diverse wildlife both above and below the water. Includes snorkelling with sharks, rays, turtles and sealions. Enquire about conditions before booking to avoid disappointment. Paddleboarding with Penguins Take a trip by paddleboard or kayak around Puerto Villamil bay to spot blue-footed boobies, penguins, marine iguanas, turtles, sea lions and golden rays.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 54 / 03. Local Information Santa Cruz Academy Bay, Puerto Ayora Location:\t00°44’53”S 90°18’34”W Entry Formalities Arranged through the World ARC agent. Mooring The World ARC fleet will be at anchor in Academy Bay. A stern anchor may help keep the boat into the southerly swells. Water Delivered by boat to your yacht, expect to pay approx. US$1 per US Gallon. Pay cash on delivery. Facilities Puerto Ayora, Santa Cruz Island, is the centre of the tourist industry in the Galapagos. Most of the day trips and longer cruises leave from this island. There is nowhere to moor a dinghy ashore, just a drop off dock, so most people use the water taxis. If you choose to use your own dinghy, it is a requirement to wear life jackets. The local maritime authority does enforce this from time to time. All the water taxis carry life jackets or buoyancy aids for each person to use when aboard.\nA to Z of Boat Services Repair facilities in Puerto Ayora, although limited, are surprisingly good for such a small place. Local mechanics being extremely adept at making emergency repairs.\nGalapagos Boat Services +593 999 145043 Offers a range of yacht services including sail repair, electronics, refrigeration, mechanical and minor rigging. Contact Jaime Yanez.\nBoatyard and Haul-out The only haul-out facility in the islands is a drying grid in the inner harbour in Puerto Ayora. This can be used depending on the state of the tides. Get permission from the Port Captain.\nElectrical and Electronics Taller Naval Yanez (see Mechanical).\nFibreglass / GRP Repair Alberto Vera Av. Bolivar Naveda Local boat builder.\nTaller Naval Yanez (see Mechanical).\nGalapagos wildlife Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 55 © World Cruising Club 2026 Fuel Quantities are calculated in US Gallons. Delivery arrangements and cost to be confirmed. Payment in advance. Be prepared with spare jerry cans if you over-estimate your requirements, as NO REFUNDS are possible. As always, filter fuel into your tank or jerry cans until you are satisfied with the quality. A permit is requested in advance and fuel will be sold at international prices that vary every month.\nIn theory, yachts larger than 30GRT are required to refuel only on Baltra Island, but, in previous years it has been possible to arrange a special delivery to Puerto Ayora for World ARC participants (for small additional charge in the region of US$0.10 per gallon).\nGas (Cooking) Mecanica Gallardo +593 525 526 6294. Propane only can be purchased for US$8/kg.\nHardware Store Locally known as ‘ferreteria’ there are plenty of hardware stores in town. Bodega Blanca is the best for boat spares, located on Seymour (open 07:00-17:00, Sat 07:30-12:30, closed Sun).\nMarine Store ElectroNautica Galapagos electronautica.com Well equipped marine store located on Charles Binford. Mechanical Taller Naval Yanez jyanezm59@yahoo.esn +593 999145043 Repair of inflatables, watermakers, refrigerators, electrician, rigging and sails. Contact: Jaime Yanez Morales on the number above.\nA to Z of What’s on Shore Accommodation Being an international port, Puerto Ayora has a good selection of hotels (Sol y Mar, Angermeyer, Finch Bay, Santa Fe, and Francis Drake Hotel). There are also several hostels. Customs Agents Note: It is not recommended to send spare parts. The courier service is expensive and slow. Gala Yachts Services Customs Agent +593 999116066 antonio@seamastersgalapagos.com ServiGalapagos S.A. info@arenas.bz +593 52526186 General Agency, fuel, permits, etc.\nDentist Molardental on Baltra Av.\nGlamSmile +593 99 774 7065 Calle San Cristobal y Rodriguez Lara. Diving There are about 30 dive sites in the Galapagos which can be accessed on day trips from Puerto Ayora. Unfortunately, you are cannot simply go and dive off your boat using your own gear. You must be accompanied by a professional bilingual naturalist and dive master. Dives with a reputable company average about US$200 per day (two dives). PADI dive courses are also available. Ice Available at the supermarket as well as most of the corner shops. Approx. US$2 for 2kg of ice.\nFishing boats in Santa Cruz Doctors / Hospitals Emergency ambulance: 911 Dr Gabriel Idrovo +593 448 8231 Private Doctor who speaks English. Expect to pay a consultation fee of $50.\nHospital Republica de Ecuador +593 2527439 Avda. Baltra on the sea front. Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 56 / 03. Local Information Internet Most bars, restaurants and cafes in town provide WiFi service. Please be aware the service can be very slow especially when many devices are linked to the same router.\nLaundry There are two laundry shops on Charles Binford which is just before the fish market on Av. Charles Darwin. They offer a same day service. Price is based on weight and turnaround time (US$1.50 per kilo). Money Banco del Pacifico bancodelpacifico.com Av. Charles Darwin. Offers cash advances on Master Card and a Cirrus ATM machine.\nMost ATMs will charge a fee and withdrawal limits vary.\nPharmacy There are plenty of pharmacies in town. The best stores can be found on Av. Baltra and several Cruz Azul blue buildings can be located near to the dock end of the avenue. They tend to stay open late into the evening.\nRestaurants Limón y Café Good for cocktails Bahia Mar Seafood and wine bar located on Av. Charles Darwin overlooking the bay. Open for lunch dinner and drinks. Midori Sushi and local dishes Galapagos Deli European style sandwiches, freshly baked pizza and homemade ice-cream. The Rock Thai style food also good for breakfast and coffee.\n1835 Coffee Lab Great for coffee and lunch .\nGolden Prague Pub style bar. Good for lunch and dinner.\nRubbish There is an official barge (green in colour) which will collect rubbish directly from your yacht. Pay in cash, depending on the amount of rubbish you have. Expect to pay approximately US$3 per bag of waste. Collections are mostly made in the morning, from 07:00 and 08:30. Try calling on Ch. 06. Shopping & Supermarkets Proinsular Supermarket Located opposite the dock. Although small by mainland standards, it does offer a good array of dried, canned, and boxed goods, as well as basic toiletries, cheeses, bottled water, and a liquor section. It opens daily including on Sundays until 12:00. Fresh produce normally arrives on the island on Saturday morning.\nBread +593 990626868 Fresh and frozen, cookies and local jams often available from a stall nearby The Rock café. Markets Town Market 1km up Av. Baltra (on the right hand side) open early on Saturday and Tuesday mornings. Good range of fresh local produce as well as fruit and vegetables from the mainland. There are also a number of stalls on the main street (and just off it) selling fruit and vegetables.\nTortuga Bay Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 57 © World Cruising Club 2026 Telephone The local operator is CLARO. Buy a sim card from any shop marked with a sticker or sign. The sim will cost roughly US$10, with US$5, US$10 and US$15 top ups available.\nTravel Information Walking is the most popular form of transport and also the cheapest. Taxis are also relatively cheap and easy to find.\nAirports Seymour/Baltra Airport (GPS). Located on Baltra Island, just north of Santa Cruz. A taxi to the Island will cost US$25 (US$30 Evening) or get a taxi to the bus station. Journey time is 30 minutes by taxi and 45 minutes by bus. After a 5 minute ferry ride (US$1) across the Baltra Chanel and a bus ride (15mins -US$5) you arrive at the airport. You do not need to pre-book the ferry and bus, they run a shuttle service back and forth.\nAirlines Airlines offering flights to the mainland. Avianca avianca.com LATAM latamairlines.com Inter-island carriers Emetebe emetebe.com.ec ESAV Airlines esavairlines.com Daily flights connecting San Cristóbal, Isabela, and Baltra – Santa Cruz.\nBicycle Rental Bikes are widely available in the town. Prices range from US$8 to US$15 per day, or US$5 to US$10 per hour. Ensure you ask in the shop where you are permitted to cycle, as some areas are restricted for walking only or completely out of bounds.\nTaxis Taxis are double-cab 4x4 jeeps. A journey within the local area will cost approx. US$1.50 per stop.\nWater Taxis AquaTaxi VHF Ch. 14. Run 24 hours a day. For early pick ups book the night before. Daytime rates are US$1 and increase after 19:00 to US$2. Luggage and or large bags may be charged extra.\nMy Notes Don’t Miss The Tortuga Bay Trail Tortuga Bay is a 40-minute walk from the water-taxi dock at Puerto Ayora. Located at the end of an easy 2.4km paved trail, Tortuga Bay has two gorgeous, powder-white sand beaches and an array of wildlife. The first beach is best for sunbathing or surfing but walk just a little further around the point, through the mangroves, and there’s a hidden beach fronting onto a calm lagoon. This beach is ideal for snorkelling and plays host to marine iguanas, turtles, pelicans, sharks and the occasional flamingo. Open from 06:00 to 17:00, it is great for runners, walkers, and suitable for prams.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 58 / 03. Local Information French Polynesia Local Information Dialling Code: +689 Time zone: UTC -9.5 (Note: The Marquesas are 30 minutes ahead of the rest of French Polynesia) Currency: CFP Franc (XPF) Emergency: 15 Language: French Useful Websites: tahititourisme.pf destinationmarquises.com tahiticruisersguide.com Entry Formalities The Customs office is co-located with the Police Station on the main high street in Atuona. All crew will need proof of health insurance that covers repatriation. After clearing in, all crew changes and boat movements must be updated in the DUP online form, as must inter-island cruises and final departure. For World ARC, these formalities will be handled by a World Cruising Club agent.\nVisas French Polynesia is an overseas department of France but is not part of the Schengen Zone. Citizens of the EU, Argentina, Australia, Canada, Chile, New Zealand, Norway, Switzerland, Mexico, Japan, UK, and USA can stay for 90 days without a specific visa. All other nationalities not mentioned are likely to require a visa in advance, which can be obtained from French diplomatic missions, for example in Panama City (pa.ambafrance.org). Passport and visa requirements are liable to change at short notice. You are advised to check entry requirements with the French Embassy or Consulate in good time, requesting specific information for entry to French Polynesia as these requirements can differ from France. Proof of Funds / Bond for non-EU Citizens These rules change often and without notice depending on officialdom’s interpretation of the law. However, in general there are two options: 1) Buy a one-way refundable airline ticket out of French Polynesia 2) Prepay an agent to guarantee a bond for you and your crew (if this option is chosen - all crew onboard will need to pay a bond regardless of whether they have an airline ticket out of French Polynesia) See noonsite.com for further information Note: The importation of plant products (such as fruit, vegetables, and flowers), as well as plants and seeds, into French Polynesia is regulated or prohibited. The movement of these products between islands is also strictly controlled.\nAll fresh fruit, vegetables, and plants on board must be declared on arrival so that the sanitary authorities can apply the appropriate measures if required. Crews are therefore encouraged to consume any fresh produce before arrival and avoid bringing plants or similar items into French Polynesia where possible, to prevent any issues with the authorities.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 59 © World Cruising Club 2026 General French Polynesia is made up of 118 islands all of which are the over-water summits of former underwater volcanoes (down to 4,000 metres deep). Volcanic islands are constantly sinking and owe their survival to coral growing on the surface. The islands are a mixture of volcanic high islands and coral atolls; the high islands have rich, fertile soil and support a much wider diversity of vegetation than the atolls. The World ARC fleet will make landfall in French Polynesia on the island of Hiva Oa in the Marquesas Islands. The arrival in Hiva Oa will be the end of the longest passage on the Rally itinerary. In addition to a warm welcome, Hiva Oa provides sufficient facilities to re-provision. The island’s windward position in the group means yachts are well placed to explore the remainder of the Marquesas. Hiva Oa is a place of pilgrimage where you can pause to reflect by the tombs of the painter Paul Gauguin and the singer Jacques Brel in the old naval cemetery of Atuona.\nThe Segalen-Gauguin museum has Gauguin works on display as well as a reconstruction of the ‘House of Pleasure’ where he lived. There is a column in homage to Jacques Brel. It is a centrepiece for artistic creativity and cultural exchanges on the island.\nMountain trails lead you to unique archaeological sites where the largest tikis (hand carved sculpture) in French Polynesia are found. The exceptional site of Puamau is well worth the extra trip.\nThe island is a hive of busy artists, wood carvers, tapa cloth makers, tattoo artists, singers and dancers. Many excursions and sights are available: waterfalls, rock outcrops, river fords or bathing in the river for nature lovers. Opening Times The opening times of business are provided as a guide and actual times may vary. Generally, businesses are available weekdays before and after lunch, and on weekend, or at least Saturday, mornings.\nMooring Boats will anchor in the NE corner of Taaoa Bay (Tahauku). The tidal range is about 1m on average. Depths vary at less than 3.5m inshore of the breakwater. The bay is open to the SE and can be quite rolly. The village of Atuona is a 30-40 minute walk around the bay to the West.\nWater There are taps on the dock to fill water containers. Although locals drink this water, it is not recommended for visitors to drink - particularly after heavy rainfall.\nMake sure to pay the fee for water usage at the City Hall.\nHiva Oa Taaoa Bay (Tahauku) Location:\t09°48’16”S 139° 01’58”W Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 60 / 03. Local Information A to Z of Boat Services Services and supplies are very limited. The closest good yacht services are in Tahiti. Limited spares are also available in Nuku Hiva.\nBoatyard & Haulout Maintenance Marquises Services (MMS) maintenancemarquises.com +689 87739045 Can lift Max 18 tonnes. Mechanical repairs, rigging, welding, electrical, sail repairs. There is also a small chandlery open Mon-Fri 07:00-16:00. Chandlery Quincaillerie Duhal A hardware store located near the Tourist Office which stocks basic household items, fishing tackle and has a small chandlery section. Mon-Fri 07:30-12:00; 14:30- 17:00; Sat 07:30-12:00; Sun 08:30-11:00. Electricity Domestic electricity points ashore are usually 240v 50Hz. European 2 round pin plugs.\nFuel There is a fuel station near the dock, however, there is no long hose, so refuelling is done by jerry can and dinghy. Fuel should be available tax free on presentation of the Tax Free Fuel Permit, purchased during clearance procedures. Open Mon-Fri 07:30-17:00 non-stop & Sat 07:00-11:30. (See more on page 61) Gas (Cooking) Gas supply is limited and Nuku Hiva is more likely to be able to supply butane. Propane is unlikely before Tahiti. Fittings are the European system.\nA to Z of What’s on Shore Accommodation Hanakee Lodge hotelhanakee.com Above Tahauku Bay Relais Moehau relaismoehau.pf Overlooking Atuona Bay and Motu Hanake.\nPension Kanahau +689 40917131/ pensionkanahau@mail.pf +689 8770126 Above Tahauku Bay (Mme. Local Information / 63 © World Cruising Club 2026 General Nuku Hiva is the largest of the Marquesas Islands in French Polynesia, and is a good option for supplies and exploring.\nThe island is rich in history. It was formerly also known as Île Marchand and Madison Island. Herman Melville wrote his book Typee based on his experiences in the Taipivai Valley in the eastern part of Nuku Hiva. Robert Louis Stevenson’s first landfall on his voyage on the Casco was at Hatihe’u, on the north side of the island, in 1888.\nThe island has several anchorages with the options on the south side of the island being most convenient. Aside from Taioha’e Bay, there are three others, at Taipivai, Ho’oumi, and Hakaui, which are part of the larger Baie du Contrôleur. Another two, at Hakau’i and Hakatea, are both accessed by the same entrance.\nA to Z of Boat Services Nuku Hiva Yacht Services yachtservicesnukuhiva.com Offers a range of services from cooking gas resupply to chandlery items. Local Information / 65 © World Cruising Club 2026 Tahiti Local Information Dialling Code: +689 Time zone: UTC -10 Currency: CFP Franc (XPF) Emergency: Police 17 Fire 18 Health 15 Coast Guard 16 Language: French and Tahitian Useful Websites: tahititourisme.org tahiticruisersguide.com Tahiti is the largest island in French Polynesia, and is one of the Windward Society Islands. It is a mountainous island with deep valleys and luscious vegetation and is made up of Tahiti Nui, the big island and Tahiti Iti, her discrete little sister. Papeete, the Capital of Tahiti, is a bustling international point of arrival with a colourful market, art and history museums, botanical and flower gardens, and animated nightlife.\nPilotage Information Charts 1382 Approaches to Tahiti and Moorea 998 Makemo to Tahiti Entry Formalities All skippers/captains MUST call the Port Control Traffic Tower (Vigi Du Port) to get authorization from the port authorities on VHF Channel 12: ● Before entering or exiting the pass of Papeete (call 10 minutes before entry or exit); ● Before mooring at Papeete Yachts Quay Pontoons or Papeete Marina; ● Before any movement in the Papeete harbour area; ● Before crossing the east and west side of the airport runway ends. Call 5 minutes before each crossing. Wait for the “green light” from Harbour Control. Having your AIS on and transmitting also helps Port Control with the airport crossing; ● Before using the Lagoon channel, going to, or coming from Marina Taina area (forbidden at night).\nAfter clearing in all crew changes and boat moves must be reported to the Maritime Office of Immigration (PAF), as must inter-island cruises and final departure details. For World ARC participants these formalities will be handled using a local agent, who must be provided with the same information in advance by email Visas See previous section on Hiva Oa.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 66 / 03. Office open Mon-Fri 07:30-16:00, Sat-Sun 10:30-16:00.\nMarina Taina Has 529 Pontoon berths on secure gated pontoons with electricity, water and rubbish disposal. 24hr security provided. 186 Mooring buoys in the lagoon nearby. Fuel pontoon. Office Open Mon–Sun 08:00–12:00, 13:00-17:00.\nA to Z of Boat Services Boatyard and Haulout Boatyard Tahiti has some of the best repair facilities in the South Pacific Islands and although labour charges are high, a specialist can be found for most jobs. There are several workshops in Fare Ute on the east side of the harbour specialising in yacht repair and maintenance. There is also a slipway and small boatyard doing hull repairs. Additionally a small shipyard is now operating out of Taravao.\nTechnimarine technimarine.pf Offering maintenance and repair services, 7250m² service area with boat lift facilities up to 300 tonnes and two additional lifts of 70 and 75 tonnes. Chandlery A good selection of chandlers in the main port on the quay “Fare Ute”. Nauti-Sport Fare Ute nautisport.pf Nauti-sport is an agent for many leading brands including Volvo Penta, Plastima, International, Garmin, Zodiac and Suzuki. Electricity European three pin plugs. Plenty of sockets on the docks. 220v 60Hz supply.\nA B Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 67 © World Cruising Club 2026 Electrical and Electronics Poly Naval Farre Ute +689 40545280 Electro Marine Services emstahiti@gmail.com +689 87 313697 Contact Thomas Dumont. SOPOM (see Mechanical) Fibreglass / GRP Repair Christian Fuller chfuller@mail.pf +689 40483601/+689 87790606 Woodworking projects, including fine interior woodwork, teak decks, varnishing, all fibreglass repair and spray painting. Installation and repair of small refrigeration units (12v-24v). English speaking. Will travel to your boat!\nFuel +689 40 433606 Duty free fuel is available at Marina Taina, five miles west of Papeete. Open Mon–Fri 06:30-12:00, 13:00-16:00, Sat 06:30-17:00, Sun 06:30-16:00. It is also open at the weekends and on holidays - however, duty free rates will not apply during these times. It is possible to arrange a duty free fuel permit which enables yachts to buy duty free fuel throughout French Polynesia. It does take up to one week from arrival to get the permit, as it must be issued by customs services in Papeete however the permit can be sent to Hiva Oa for collection from the World ARC agent. This provides a saving when using over 450 litres during your stay in French Polynesia. Duty free fuel is available in Hiva Oa, Nuku Hiva (bigger quantity in stock), Rangiroa (Tuamotu), Tahiti and all Leeward Islands (Raiatea, Bora Bora, Huahine and Tahaa). Gas (Cooking) Gaz de Tahiti Zones des Hydrocarbures +689 40508400 In Fare Ute, situated on a turn off on the road to the Port Authority. Open Mon-Fri 07:30-12:00 & 13:00-16:00. Most types of containers filled, including Camping Gaz. Butane only available through the World ARC Agent. Gas bottle refilling is not commercially available in Bora Bora. Ideally yachts requiring gas should refill bottles before leaving Tahiti. Propane bottles must be filled in Tahiti. Hardware Store Marine related stores are found on Quay Fare Ute, others in downtown Papeete.\nAce Sin Tung Hing In Fare Ute. Open Mon-Sat, closed Sundays.\nMr. Bricolage Tahiti +689 40506550 Between Marina Taina and the airport. Open: Mon-Sat 07:30-17:30.\nLiferaft Servicing Marine Corail Fare Ute +689 40428222 Contact Francois Charpentier.\nNauti-Sport Fare Ute nautisport.pf +689 40533070 Mechanical Societe Polynesien Moteurs (SOPOM) sopom.pf Located between Nautisport and the bridge. Open Mon-Sat. Sales and service of: marine diesel engines (Nanni die-sel, Baudouin), turbines (Castoldjet, Hamiltonjet), gensets (Sdmo, Genelec, Nanni diesel), watermakers (SLCE, Aquaset). Also: propeller shafts, propellers, hydrojet, alternators, inverters, fans, air extractors, filters, rubber couplers, flexible hoses, thermal & sound insulation, fishing gear, winches and more. Can take care of maintenance and repairs (more than 15,000 parts in stock).\nPolynesie Marine +689 40 58 47 47 Tool shop and Tohatsu outboard repair centre. Metalwork / Welding Faure Naval +689 40782983 Repairs & Welding +689 40796089 Next to Stade Central, zone industrielle de Tiperui. Refrigeration Christian Fuller (see Fibreglass Repair) Sails & Rigging Polynesia Rigging Services polynesiariggingservices.com Contact Jean Christophe Grimaldi Tahiti Sails tahitisails.com Repairs, new sails, upholstery and other canvas projects.\nNauti-Sport (see Chandlery) Watermakers Sopomeca Fare Ute. +689 40451055 SOPOM (see Mechanical) Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 68 / 03. Local Information A to Z of What’s on Shore Business hours for all shops are usually Mon-Fri 08:00-11:30 & 13:30-17:30.\nAccommodation Many of Tahiti’s hotels, are amongst some of the most unique in the world. Most offer accommodation in individual thatched ‘fare’ (bungalows) located in tropical gardens, along beaches, and over water on stilts. The budget end of the market comes in the form of “Pensions de Famille”, the Tahitian version of Bed and Breakfast.\nBook Shop Te Fare Tahiti Nui (Public Cultural Centre) maisondelaculture.pf Public books, video & CD library. Lots of books about all aspects of French Polynesia.\nDiving Tahiti Plongee Passion tahitiplongeepassion.com PADI Dive Resort to the east of Pape’ete.\nEleuthera Plongee eleutheratahiti.com PADI Dive Resort to the south of Fa’a’ā.\nInternet The main provider is VINI. Vinispot Wi-Fi cards are available to purchase for 1, 5, 10, 50 & 100 hours with unlimited download data within these periods. Local Sim Cards can be purchased in the Post Office – recharge vouchers are available in most supermarkets.\nIce Available at supermarkets. Laundry Laverie Du Pont de l’est +689 87781959 Rua Paul Gaugin 1. Open Mon–Fri 07:00-16:00, Sat 07:00-12:00. Expect to pay XPF2500 for a 7kg wash, dry and fold. 24 hour turnaround. Marina Taina has 24hr self service machines, located in the same building as the marina office.\nMoney There are many banks along the main front street Boulevard Pomare, which all have ATMs and money changing facilities. Open Mon-Fri 07:00-15:30; closed weekends.\nFor Marina Taina, the closest ATMs can be found at the Mobil service station and Carrefour supermarket, just outside the marina.\nCredit cards are widely used throughout the islands.\nPharmacy Pharmacie du Port pharmacieduport.shop +689 40420069 Closest pharmacy to the dock. Pharmacie Lafayette Moana Nui pharmacielafayettepf.com Located in the Moana Nui Shopping Centre. Post Office www.opt.pf Boulevard Pomare, next to Parc Bougainville. Open Mon-Fri 07:30-17:00; Sat 07:30-11:00. Restaurants French Polynesia is renowned for its unique cuisine using traditional cooking methods combined with French gastronomy, and Italian and Chinese influences. This can be sampled at the many sophisticated restaurants and at the cheap road side mobile snack bars. Be sure to try the delectable national dish, ‘poisson cru’, fresh fish marinated in lime juice and fresh coconut milk. Along Papeete’s waterfront and scattered throughout Tahiti you will find colourful vans that have been converted into mobile restaurants called ‘les roulottes’. Usually family run, they cook a variety of chicken, steak, fish, waffles, crêpes, pizza and other substantial meals often cooked over gas fires and are excellent value.\nRubbish Organised by Yacht Harbour and Marina Taina.\nDoctors / Hospitals Emergency ambulance: 15 Centre Hospitalier de Polynésie Française chpf.pf Main hospital with 24 hour casualty department situated at the west end of the city along Rue Georges Clemenceau. Payment must be made in full for most treatment.\nClinique Paofai polyclinique-paofai.pf A private clinic close to the yacht quay with good facilities. Cardella Clinic cardella.pf/ Near the cathedral. Medical facilities are of a high standard.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 69 © World Cruising Club 2026 Shopping & Supermarkets Supermarkets Carrefour Supermarket carrefour.pf 5 minutes walk from Marina Taina. Open Mon-Sun 08:00-20:00. Champion Supermarket championtahiti.com Situated on the Rue de Commandant Destremeau, close to Pape’ete Marina. Open Mon–Sat 0600-1900; Sun 06:00-12:00.\nMarket The central market offers a range of goods from fresh vegetables and fish to tourist goods. Open Mon-Sat 07:30-18:00; Sun 05:00-09:00. Shopping Centres Moana Nui Shopping Centre Home to the large Carrefour supermarket, banks, and Post Office. Vaima Centre centrevaima.com Largest shopping mall in French Polynesia.\nTourist Office tahititourisme Situated in the park on the waterfront by the Quai d’Honneur. Information on all of French Polynesia. Open Mon-Fri 07:30-17:00; Sat 08:00-16:00; Sun 08:00-13:00.\nYacht Clubs Tahiti Yacht Club yctahiti.org Visiting yachts welcome, but limited facilities for large numbers. Travel Information Airports Faa’a International Airport (PPT) tahiti-aeroports.com 3km from downtown Papeete. Airlines International Flights Air France airfrance.pf Air New Zealand airnewzealand.pf Air Tahiti Nui airtahitinui.com French Bee frenchbee.com United Airlines united.com Inter Island Flights Air Tahiti airtahiti.com Air Moana airmoana.com Buses There are many buses serving Papeete with nearly all routes commencing and stopping near the market in the downtown area. Using buses is easy and cheap [approx. XPF200 per journey]. There are designated stops all along the circular island road, however, in many cases the bus will stop anywhere when signalled. There is a bus stop on both sides of the road outside Marina Taina. Service hours are early morning through to late afternoon/early evening, Monday to Friday inclusive, although on the two major East and West routes, services generally run later into the evenings with a limited weekend service.\nCar Rental Driving is on the right hand side. Stay clear of driving into town between 06:00-08:00 and out of town between 16:00-17:30. If hiring a motorbike we recommend you refrain from riding after dark as there are very few street lights. Most hire car companies are based at Faa’a Airport (Avis, Europcar and Hertz).\nTahiti Island Rentals tahitiislandrentals.com Next to Marina Taina. Cars and scooters available, good English.\nTaxis These can be expensive, though regulation of tariffs and the installation of meters in many taxis has seen prices reduced. Prices are usually displayed within the taxi, if you are unsure about the cost ask the driver before starting the journey. There is a taxi rank across the road from the entrance to the Marina de Papeete. Rangiroa Blue Lagoon. Photo Credit: Jayne Ogilvie Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 70 / 03. Local Information Don’t Miss Traditional Tahitian food Throughout Tahiti and its islands, food is still cooked in traditional pit ovens. The food (most likely local pork) is wrapped in banana leaves and placed in a hole in the ground on top of stones that have been heated in a fire, then covered with earth. The steaming process takes several hours resulting in mouth watering food.\nExplore inland Beyond the white fringe of the beaches exists a heart born of volcanic upheaval: Faatautia Valley is unspoiled and lovely, the Arahoho Blowholes show the power of the ocean, and the lava tubes of Hitiaa are a maze of natural tubes through which you can walk or swim, filled with grottoes, waterfalls, streams and caves.\nAncient history On Tahiti and Moorea alone there are hundreds of ancient marae, which mark sacred Polynesian ground, hidden under the shade of the green canopy. It is on these ancient platforms that the Polynesian culture we see today was born. Getting to some of these places requires four-wheel-drive vehicles or a good set of hiking shoes — and sometimes both.\nDiving With water that often exceeds 200 feet in visibility, divers come from all over the globe to see Tahiti’s sharks (silvertip, blacktip reef, whitetip, lemon and grey), acres of hard-coral kingdoms, stingrays, turtles, mantas and clownfish galore.\nPearls The naturally black Tahitian pearls are reputed to be the most valuable in the world due to their exotic colours and rarity.\nMy Notes Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 71 © World Cruising Club 2026 Bora Bora Bora Bora Yacht Club Bora Bora Yacht Club, BP 123 VAITAPE, 98730 Bora Bora.\nLocation:\t16°29’22”S 151°45’39”W VHF: Ch.09 T: +689 40676047 Email: contact.bbyc@gmail.com Website: facebook.com/yachtclubborabora Water: Potable Pilotage Information Charts 1060 Muahine to Maupiti 1107 Plans in the Society Island General Bora Bora is one of the Leeward Society Islands and some would say, the most beautiful island in the Pacific. Dominating the island are the volcanic peaks of Mount Otemanu and Mount Pahia. Separated from the principal island by a deep lagoon, is the small island of Toopua and the islet of Toopua Iti. The coast of the main island is bordered by a fringing reef that disappears in the southern part to give way to the magnificent white sand beaches of Taahana and Matira.\nThe Island’s ancient name of Vava’u suggests that the original inhabitants of island arrived from Tonga. Interestingly, in the local Tahitian language there is no “B,” so its actual name is then Pora Pora, meaning “first born.” Entry Formalities Customs are located in the Gendarmerie opposite the city dock on the main road. Visas See previous section on Hiva Oa.\nMooring bbmoorings@gmail.com The Bora Bora Yacht Club is just inside the only channel entering the lagoon with 25 moorings available managed by Bora Bora Moorings Services.\nBBYC offers a day pass at XFP3000/day that can be redeemed on a daily basis from your dinner at the Yacht Club Restaurant (food only). This gives you access to the dinghy dock, showers (cold water only), rubbish, parking for scooters, car or bike and Wi-Fi at the restaurant.\nNote: Anchoring is prohibited in Bora Bora and mooring balls must be used. See noonsite.com and further information will be provided by Rally Control. Water Potable water is available at Vaitape Pier. Purchase prepaid card from Bora Bora Mooring Services (VHF Ch.09 / +689 89440 888). Or purchase from BBYC via hose or by jerry cans. Cost is XPF1,000 per 100 litres. Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 72 / 03. Local Information A to Z of Boat Services Boatyard and Haulout There are no repair or haul-out facilities in Bora Bora. The nearest are in Raiatea: Raiatea Carenage Services raiateacarenage.com Call on VHF Ch. 68 when near Raiatea.\nChandlery Marine Services ms@mail.pf On the road between Mai Kai Marina and Vaitape, opposite the supermarket Super To’A Amok. Open Mon-Fri 07:30-12:00 & 13:30-16:30; Sat 08:00-12:00. Stocks basic parts and can carry out basic work if needed. QBB Chandlery +689 40 675290 Next door to supermarket Amok. Mon-Fri 07:00-17:00 Sat 07:00-12:00.\nFuel The TOTAL fuel station is situated south of the Yacht Club in the bay before Vaitape. There is duty free fuel for visiting yachts that have the right paperwork (for French Polynesia) - see note below. Approach can be done whenever the dock is free without needing to notify them. The fuel dock has a 3m draft. As an alternative, boats can either moor stern-to the dock or, for bigger boats, it is possible to approach Farepiti dock at Fa’anui bay, to the north, and arrange a fuel truck to visit. (Contact Alain T: +689 87782781) Note: Requirements for duty free fuel: “Export” (vessels fuelling up just before leaving the country) must present a copy of: ● “Autorisation d’approvisionnement”; ● Boat registration; ● “Déclaration d’arrivée”; ● “Déclaration en douane”; ● Permis Sortie/Clearance. (Both 4 and 5 are acquired only after clearing out of the country with Customs & Immigration).\n“Plaisance” (vessels cruising the country and not leaving yet) must present a copy of: ● “Autorisation d’approvisionnement”; ● Boat registration; ● “Déclaration d’arrivée”.\nGas (Cooking) Gas bottle refilling is not commercially available in Bora Bora. Ideally yachts requiring gas should refill bottles before leaving Tahiti. Propane bottles must be filled in Tahiti. Raiatea Yacht Carenage Service Can refill butane bottles including Camping Gaz bottles. They are closed on Sundays. Please contact in advance on VHF Ch 68 or+689 40600545 Closest pharmacy to the dock. NOTE: Some boats resort to gravity filling which is dangerous and not allowed to be done on-site.\nMechanical Marine Services (see Chandlery) Sells outboards and stock a limited range of spare parts. A to Z of What’s on Shore Accommodation Most accommodation is on the south coast. There is a full range from five-star luxury hotels to cheaper smaller pensions. Maitra Point, next to the best beach on Bora Bora, is packed with a full range of accommodation.\nDentist Available at the medical centre.\nDr Zona +689 40676942 Dr Macouin and Dr Maton +689 40677055 Doctors / Hospitals Emergency ambulance: 15 The nearest hospital is in Raiatea, and for serious conditions Tahiti.\nCentre Medical de Bora Bora ww+689 4067 6366 Dr Azad Roussanaly +689 4067 7795 Dr Duval +689 4067 6707 Dr Maria Lopez +689 4060 3636 Dr Juen and Semararo (at Nunue) +689 4067 7062 Dr Krajewski +689 4067 6737 Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. XPF3000 per load for wash, dry and fold. Open Mon, Tue, Wed & Fri 07:30-17:30.\nMoney There are three banks in Vaitape, two of them very close to the main dock.\nSocredo Bank (closest to Bora-Bora Marina) Mon-Fri 07:30-11:30 & 13:00-15:30 Banque de Polynésie Mon-Thu 07:45-11:45 & 12:45-16:15; Fri 07:45-11:45 & 12:45-15:15 Banque de Tahiti Mon-Fri 08:00-12:00 & 13:00-16:00.\nPharmacy Pharmacie Lafayette Bora Bora pharmacielafayette.com Near to the Chin Lee Supermarket (see shopping) and Le Cocotier Restaurant.
