Restaurants A few restaurants offer a free pick up service St James Bora Bora Vaitape saintjamesborabora.com +689 40 676462 Bora Bora Yacht Club Viatape +689 40 676047 Bora Bora Beach Club Matira Beach boraborabeachclub@gmail.com +689 40 675 250 Rubbish Bora Bora Yacht Club Rubbish disposal facilities behind the building in the car park available outside these hours 11:30-14:00, 17:30-21:00. Green container for cans/plastic recycling (dispose of rubbish without bags); black for general waste.\nBora Bora Mooring Services Takes rubbish on Saturday & Tuesday mornings direct from you boat call on VHF Ch.09 or +689 89440 888. Please separate and recycle where possible. Max of two bags per boat.\nShopping & Supermarkets There are two main well-stocked supermarkets. Super U To’a Amok Mon-Sat 05:30-18:30.\nChin Lee Supermarket Opposite the Vaitape shopping centre. Open daily 05:00-20:00.Browse arts and crafts from Bora Bora at i te fanau tahi. Situated in a big building right at the main square of town, by the public dock. Open daily.\nTours Matira Jet Tours matirajettours.com Tours - Jet Ski, SUP/Kayak and Quad/ATV.\nPolynesia Island Tours +689 87 296 660 Bora Bora offers plenty of great walking tours including the ascent of Mount Pahia.\nWater Taxi / Lagoon Tours Manu Taxi Boat fanfantaxiboat@mail.pf +689 87791162 Boat tours including excursion in the resort-free Southeastern part of the island. Snorkeling, manta ray and shark feeding expeditions, traditional lunch at the beach of Motu Fanfan.\nLagoon Service lagoonservice.com Comfortable boats and various lagoon tours with swimming, snorkeling and lunch. Moana Adventure Tours moanaadventuretours.com Offering local, certified guides and various combo tour options.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 74 / 03. Local Information Reef Discovery r reefdiscovery.com Specialises in small groups interested in manta rays and whales. Certified naturalist guides. Gear, lessons and boats provided. Tourist Information +689 677 636 Located on the main dock. Open Mon-Fri 08:30-12:30, 13:30-16:30, Sat 08:30-12:30. Travel Information Airports Bora Bora Airport (BOB) On Motu Mute, a palm fringed motu approximately 20 minutes by motor launch from the main village of Vaitape. Two large catamaran ferries operate a free transfer to the Vaitape Quay from where there is a regular bus service to Matira Point. Airlines Inter Island Flights Air Tahiti airtahiti.com Operate six flights per day between Tahiti and Bora Bora.\nAir Moana airmoana.com Bicycle Rental Avis Bora Bora avis-borabora.com Hire bikes and ebikes cost from XFP2100 a day.\nBuses There is no bus service in town. When cruise ships are in, there are some private local buses (called “trucks”) going south, which serve anyone else interested.\nCar Rental Avis Bora Bora avis-borabora.com Hire cars, scooters and buggies. A Fiat Panda costs XFP11,900 a day. Albert Rent a Car +689 40677555/+689 8778466 Bora Bora Fun Tours +689 87727433 At Maitai Matira, Southeast of the main island. Taxis Taxis congregate at the main dock In Vaitape.\nHeimana Tours (Julia & Léon) heimanatours@hotmail.fr +689 87706916 +689 87768605 Dora Taxi +689 8973 4957 Dany TARUOURA +689 8771 7911 Jeremie +689 8770 6916 Don’t Miss Col de Faanui The ultimate viewpoint, a medium level five hour hike with unforgettable views. The Tourist Information Office advises that visitors hire a guide for this hike, to assure the safety and good experience for all. There is a very interesting trekking tour with a mountaineer-archaeologist in town that speaks English.\nTour of the lagoon There are many different types of tours, including outrigger speed canoe, glass bottomed boats and helicopter “flight” seeing!\nSealife Due to a recent drop in manta ray population (over 80%), all cruising yachts are requested not to attempt to snorkel in the lagoon without a guide. Snorkellers can see almost as much as divers, and some companies also offer the chance to observe rays and sharks feeding. However simply looking down into the water from a boat results in easy views of countless species of tropical fish, brilliant coral gardens and the lagoon’s larger residents, including rays.\nA Motu picnic Nothing beats watching the sun go down over the reef from one of the outlying sandy atolls (Motu).\nCycling around the Island One of the best ways to get around this tiny island is by bicycle. The road is paved, circles the island [32km], and is predominantly flat. Bora Bora has several ancient temples (marae) and authentic Polynesian villages. There are also some reminders of World War II with a number of cannon emplacements left behind by the U.S. Navy.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 75 © World Cruising Club 2026 Niue Local Information Dialling Code: +683 Time zone: UTC -11 Currency: NZ$ Emergency: 999 Language: English and Niuean Useful Websites: niueisland.com Niue is one of the world’s smallest self-governing states, and rules in free association with New Zealand. Most Niueans now live in New Zealand, and the island itself only has a population of about 1500.\nThe Island is a raised atoll of rocky limestone - unlike any of its neighbours - with rocky seashores, gaping sea caves and the most spectacular, an extensive, cave system in the entire South Pacific! The pace of life in Niue is relaxed, shops close at 16:00 and Sundays are strictly a day of rest - fishing, diving and boating are not permitted. The Niueans are proud that their Island is one of the safest in the World- crime, graffiti, drugs and pollution almost unheard of. Traditions are respected and continue to be taught at school. Entry Formalities Formalities will take place at the wharf and will be coordinated through Rally Control. Niue Radio may ask some questions on VHF as you approach your mooring.\nOn departure, a tax of NZ$150 per person is required payable by cash or card. Children under 12 are exempt.\nVisas There are no visa requirements for stays of up to 30 days.\nMooring Safe moorings are put down every season by the Niue Yacht Club for visiting yachts, available for around NZ$30per night. All moorings are regularly inspected. The moorings are approx 4.8 tonne concrete blocks with new polyester lines and pink pick up floats. Pass your rope through the stainless steel eye, tie off on board and put the float back in the water. Anchoring is also possible, but be careful of the sharp drop off from the edge of the suitable shelf, which can be difficult. Landing with your dinghy can be an interesting experience as there is no beach, just a concrete jetty. Your tender must be lifted out by the dock Niue Yacht Club Moorings Location:\t19° 3’11.80”S, 169°55’23.16”W VHF: Ch.16 Email: nyc@niueisland.com Website: niueisland.com/niue-yacht-club Water: Potable Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 76 / 03. Local Information crane onto the wharf, where there is a trolley for moving it clear of the landing zone. You will need a three point bridle for your dinghy.\nWater All water on the island is safe to drink. Two outlets are available on the wharf, you just have to bring the jerry cans.\nA to Z of Boat Services Very limited. Minor repairs to diesel and outboard engines. Aluminum welding of spars available. Given sufficient notice, specific spares can be flown in from New Zealand, on the weekly flight.\nElectricity 240 volts, 10 amps, 2 pin plug, same as New Zealand.\nFuel Diesel or petrol is available from Central Services. Delivery to the wharf can be arranged for a small additional fee. Yachts must supply their own fuel containers. Bulk refuelling is also possible, with the yacht moored alongside the wharf for as long as it takes to transfer fuel as long as the swell conditions permit (not advised).\nGas (Cooking) Depending on adaptor, propane gas is available. Speak to Rally Control on arrival. If in desperate need, new gas bottles can be purchased.\nHardware Store Ali’s, BJ’s or Swanson’s. Limited stocks.\nA to Z of What’s on Shore Accommodation A full range from the Matavai Resort to guesthouses and hostels are available.\nDiving There are many excellent dive highlights. For non-divers you can swim with spinner dolphins or snorkel with humpback whales.\nNiue Blue niueblue.com Offer snorkeling and scuba tours, rental and courses. Magical Niue magicalniue.com Hairdressers Sala’s Salon For women and men’s haircutting at the commercial center.\nVilina Hair & Beauty Located at Togalupo, Alofi.\nIce Niue Bond Shop, Central Services, and if re-opened, Niue Fish Processors on the wharf.\nInternet Telecom Niue telecomniue.com 4G data Sim Cards available to purchase.\nNiue Dinghy Hook Doctors / Hospitals Emergency ambulance: 999 Niue Foou Hospital +683 4100 facebook.com/niuefoou.hospital.3 Located near the airport. New hospital with dental facilities, X-ray, ultrasound and lab technicians. Visitors pay a modest fee for doctors’ visits and medication. Medivac arrangements for serious medical cases to NZ. Clinic opening hours: Mon-Thu 0800-1600, Fri, Sat Sun & Holidays 09:00-10:00 & 19:00-20:00 Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 77 © World Cruising Club 2026 Laundry Hinas Laundry Behind Swanson Supermarket. NZ$25 per load wash and dry and NZ$15 per load for wash only. Open Mon-Fri 08:00–16:00.\nLaundry 200m from the wharf. NZ$25 per load wash and dry. Money There are no ATM machines, but money may be exchanged at the Bank of South Pacific Kiwi Bank, located in Alofi. The bank is a short walk from the wharf. Many establishments take credit cards but please check with restaurants and services etc. before booking.\nPharmacy At the Hospital. Panadol and flu tablets are also available at the supermarkets.\nPost Office In Alofi Commercial Centre. Post in and out once per week. Mon-Fri 09:00-15:30. Outward mail closes Thu 13:00 for Fri morning flight out. It is not recommended to mail anything to Niue.\nRestaurants A wide range of restaurants are available, see niueisland.com/see-and-do/eating-and-drinking for full list!\nCrazy Uga Cafe +683 4653 Main Street, Alofi. Mon-Fri 08:00-14:00, Wed 18:00-21:00, Sat 09:00-14:00. Real Expresso Available! Light lunches and dinner. Fully licensed bar. Cash only Kaiika Gourmet Cuisine & Avi’s Ark kaiika.com Alofi South. Offering authentic Japanese cuisine - sushi, sashimi and tempura. Kaiika also offers homemade pizza. Avi’s Ark serves lunch and great ice cream with fantastic sea views. Manuiz Café Bar facebook.com/manuiz.halaika A small family restaurant and bar located 10 mins walk from the wharf. Also home to the Niue Yacht Club. Tavana’s Café +683 4334 Alofi Commercial Centre. Mon-Fri 09:00-14:00, Burger Night Sat: 18:00-20:00.\nScenic Matavai Resort Open 7 days a week for breakfast, lunch and dinner. Buffet on Thursdays from 1900 and BBQ on Fridays Sundays from 1830. Booking essential.\nVaiolama Café & Restaurant facebook.com/VaiolamaCafe Alofi South - stunning licensed café with view of the Pacific Ocean. Vanilla Café Bar & Restaurant https://www.facebook.com/profile.\nphp?id=100090742330152 Open Mon/Tues/Fri 09:00-14:30/17:00-23:00, plus Saturday and Sunday evenings.\nRubbish Land at the wharf. The cost is NZ$25 per boat.\nRubbish area in a little blue outhouse just up the hill from the wharf.\nShopping Markets Wednesday and Friday mornings (06:00) for fresh produce and traditionally prepared dishes along with local handicrafts. Friday night food markets are held on a fortnightly basis, and an organic market on the first Thursday of the month from 17:00. Supermarkets Swanson’s facebook.com/p/Swanson-Supermarket Located in the retail hub near the airport. Open Monday to Friday 08:00 to 18:00, Saturday 10:00 to 18:00, Sunday closed. The Niue Bond Store for duty free liquor sales is also located at the retail hub. Duty free allowance for cruisers on arrival, as well as after Customs clearance. Smaller shops Outside these hours some smaller shops are open, but carry less range. These stores are often located in houses within villages – if in doubt, just ask around!\nSports Facilities Hash House Harriers +683 3001 Run/walk on Monday afternoons Niue Golf and Sports Club Compact nine hole golf course, and tennis courts. Hire Clubs from Niue Tourism Office.\nTourist Information Niue Tourism Visitor Centre niueisland.com +683 4224 Alofi’s main road. Mon-Fri 08:00-16:00, Sat-Sun 09:00-12:00.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 78 / 03. Local Information Yacht Club Niue Yacht Club niueisland.com/niue-yacht-club The Niue Yacht Club facilities and the moorings are situated on the western side of the island near the main wharf and settlement of Alofi. NYC have set up HQ at Manuiz in Alofi which is a 10-15 minute walk from the wharf.\nTravel Information Airports Hanan International Airport (IUE) 2km south-east of the main town of Alofi. Taxi or Airport Transfer should be booked in advance through your accommodation or contact Niue Tourism Visitor Centre.\nAirlines Air New Zealand airnewzealand.co.nz/destination-niue Operates two flights per week to and from Aukland, every Saturday and Tuesday.\nBicycle Rental Niue is a good place to cycle, with a 68km ring road around the upraised atoll. Mountain bikes for hire for around NZ$20/day. Niue Blue (see Diving) rent e-bikes from NZ$50/day.\nBuses No public transport but local people will often stop and offer visitors a ride. A restaurant shuttle bus can be arranged through Matavai Resort. T: +683 4368 (NZ$5 return) Car Rental A local Niue Photo Driving Licence is required (NZ$22.50).\nAlofi Rentals alofirentals@niue.nu +683 4017 / +683 4373 Pao, Alofi South Open Mon-Fri 08:00-16:00, Sat: 09:00-12:00.\nNiue Rentals niuerentals.nu Open Mon-Fri 08:00-16:00 Ilenas Car Rentals patelesio.vea@niue.nu Taxis Mitaki Taxis +683 4084 Alofi Rentals +683 4017 Don’t Miss Niue has no poisonous animals or plants, so you can amble anywhere, anytime. Find out more at the Niue Tourism Visitor Centre in Alofi.\nRainforest walking amongst flocks of butterflies in the virgin rainforest of the Huvalu Forest Conservation Area.\nVisit some of the Island’s famous beauty spots: Togo Chasm; Talava Arches; Anapala Chasm.\nDiving within dramatic underwater cathedrals in water teeming with life, including the friendly Niuean sea snake.\nCycling beneath avenues of perfumed frangipani.\nSwimming in coves that might be yours for the whole day, like Limu and Matapa Chasm.\nExplore the most breathtaking caves in the South Pacific. The spectacular underwater caves are dwarfed by the ones you’ll be able to explore on land. They’re easier to get to as well. Some you can walk to yourself, but for the best ones you’ll need a guide. The key man is Tali Magatogia, a Niuean who’s been running very special tours for years and can take you places that will make your spine tingle. What’s more, there are so many caves within Niue that up until the mid-1800’s most people lived in them.\nSnorkeling over bright corals in crystal clear warm tropical water.\nDiving in Niue Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 79 © World Cruising Club 2026 Tonga Local Information Dialling Code: +676 Time zone: UTC +13 Currency: Tongan $ or Pa’anga (TOP) Emergency: Ambulance 999 Police 992 Fire 993 Language: Tongan and English Useful Websites: tongatourism.travel vavau.to Pilotage Information Charts NZ8234 Vava’u group Pilots Pacific Crossing Guide (RCC) Kingdom of Tonga (Hellewell) Entry Formalities All visiting yachts will need to submit an Advance Notice of Arrival form to the following 3 email addresses: info@customs.\ngov.to pooikaufusi@gmail.com & sp.niulala@ gmail.com at least 24h hours prior to arrival. Yachts must berth on main quay on arrival (if full, it is permitted to anchor or pick up a mooring) and await clearance. Yachts will be visited by all the authorities together (customs, immigration, quarantine and health); normal working hours are Mon-Fri 08:30-16:30. Customs overtime is TOP$140 out of hours, or TOP$140 on a Saturday, and closed on Sunday so it is better not to arrive over the weekend.\nImportant: Arriving yachts must have clearance from previous port, or risk a heavy fine (about TOP$2,000).\nVisas Citizens of most countries are granted a 31 day visitors visa on arrival. Passports must have a minimum of six months validity. Letter of Authority for Crew flying in Persons arriving in Tonga by air and intending to depart by yacht, MUST hold a Letter of Authority from one of Tonga’s diplomatic missions overseas and bearing the official stamp of that Tongan diplomatic mission or Letter of Authority issued by the Immigration Division, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Government of Tonga, bearing the stamp of the Principal Immigration Officer. It can also be arranged by sending an email to tongapermit@gmail.com no more than one month before arrival, proclaiming the incoming crew will be leaving by boat on a specific date. The following information will be required: ● Crew name ● Arrival date ● Date and booking number of arrival flight ● Passport number and date of expiry ● Full name ● Date of birth ● Skipper’s name ● Boat owner’s name ● Boat name, registration number, country, and port of registry An official letter will be issued to be presented by the traveller. The letter is valid for one month. A money order for TOP$34.50 is required for the one-way authorisation letter to be issued.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 80 / 03. Local Information General The Vava’u group is made up of 40 islands, of which 13 are inhabited by a population of about 16,000, offering fantastic cruising through it’s protected waterways, the smaller islands surrounded by white sands and vibrant coral reefs. Ashore the rolling countryside offers a host of activities to be enjoyed, with plenty of opportunities for a good walk to stretch your ‘sea legs’.\nThe original vegetation of Tonga was tropical rainforest, however today this has been almost completely lost throughout the Kingdom with only small remnants remaining on Tongatapu, some of the Vava’u islands and on ‘Eua. Neiafu, capital of Vava’u, is where the World ARC yachts will be based. Once ashore everything is within easy walking distance of the town centre. Tongans are welcoming, relaxed, and generally happy. Minor things like delays or noise tend not to cause any stress. Tongans enjoy the beach, collecting from the sea, singing, and dancing. Any occasion, big or small, can become a good reason for getting together to celebrate with friends and family. In Tongan life, the family is of utmost importance. Each family member plays a role, with older persons commanding the most respect. Women benefit from a higher social status than in other parts of Polynesia as tradition gives them certain authority over male family members. Public life, however, is still dominated by men. The eldest sister acts as the family matriarch and oversees her siblings, nieces, and nephews.\nReligion closely follows the family in importance, and almost all Tongans are church goers. This staunchly Christian nation honours Sundays across every island group—the Sabbath is declared forever sacred in the Tongan Constitution. Almost everything (except bakeries, a few restaurants, and resorts) is closed. No sporting events take place, it is unlawful to work or trade, and most tours do not operate. Contracts signed on a Sunday are void. Avoid creating any disturbance on Sundays.\nYour independent cruising period through the islands of Tonga and Fiji starts here with an optional leg into the Lau Group for clearance into eastern Fiji.\nNeiafu, Vava’u Location:\t18°39’8.19”S, 173°59’8.94”W VHF: Ch.16 Cruisers’ net: Mon-Sat 08:30 VHF Ch 26 +676 70319 Mooring You will be directed to berth alongside the customs wharf please note you may need to raft up to 3 boats. If there is no space then you will be advised to pick up a mooring or anchor and await clearance.\nWater Jerry cans of portable rainwater are available from Tropicana (20L = TOP$2). Bottled water is available from most shops.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 81 © World Cruising Club 2026 A to Z of Boat Services Boatyard & Haulout The Boatyard boatyardvavau.com Very good boatyard, reliable and professional. Offer a range of services including electrical mechanical and fibreglass repairs. Also has small chandlery. Just on the other side of the peninsula. Chandlery The Boatyard (see Boatyard & Haulout) Hardware stores in town EM Jones and Pacific Timber and Hardware stock a small amount of marine related items. Fuel Pacific Energy Duty Free fuel after clearing out. Please contact in advance with your order. Fuelling will take place on the northern side of the main wharf. Only available on Mon-Fri (Friday can get very busy so best avoided). Note: Fuel delivery is notoriously prone to arrive late in Neiafu, but once arrived fuel delivery is quick and efficient.\nGas (Cooking) Homegas +676 70319 (Marcella’s Resort in Toula). Local Information / 83 © World Cruising Club 2026 Travel Information Airports International flights arrive in Tongatapu at: Fua’amotu International Airport (TBU) Lupepau’u Airport (VAV) for domestic flights to Vava’u.\nAirlines Lulutai Airlines lulutai-airlines.to Operate domestic flights connecting the five main island groups of Tonga. Lulutai Airlines flies 2-3 times daily from Tongatapu domestic airport to Vava’u’s Lupepau’u Airport. Fiji Airways fijiairways.com Operates direct flights between Vava’u and Nadi, Fiji.\nBicycle Rental Tropo Rentals Based at Cafe Tropicana - see Restaurants.\nFriendly Islands Kayak Co fikco.com Runs kayak and mountain bike tours.\nCar Rental Car Hire required a Tongan license which can be obtained from the Police Station.\nCoconut Car Rental coconutcarrental@gmail.com +676 7556667 Avis reservation@avis.to Main Office: (676) 23500 Airport Location: (676) 35004 Tropicana Rental tropicana@vavau.to +676 71322/8412347 Ferries Tofa Ramsay Shipping facebook.com/tofaramsayshipping Tongan inter-island passenger and cargo services to ‘Eua, Ha’apai, Niuas, and Vava’u. There is a ferry service once a week. Its route is Tongatapu-Ha’apai-Vava’u and then back to Tongatapu. Taxis There are two taxi ranks located in the area around the ANZ bank and opposite the Police station. Be aware that taxis on Sundays may be harder to come by without an advance arrangement. Tours around Main Island will vary a lot depending on distance covered, as fuel prices here are very expensive.\nDon’t Miss Whale watching The warm seas and calm waters of Vava’u provide the perfect nursery for Southern humpback whales. Having spent their summer feeding in Antarctica, they travel thousands of miles to mate and calf in Vava’u between June and November. If you haven’t been lucky enough to see whales from your own boat, join one of the many organised operators and try your luck.\nBotanical Gardens As part of the World ARC stopover, a tour is arranged to visit the Botanical Gardens, on the North West coast of Vava’u a scenic drive away across the island. There is a great selection of plants to be seen, and the opportunity to gain insight into the development of the local vanilla growing industry. The tour then visits ‘Ene ’Io ’io beach, a lovely place to stop at for a swim. There is a great restaurant from which to enjoy magnificent views across a beautifully sheltered bay.\nKayaking and outrigger canoeing Try a different craft for some close up coastal exploration in these calm waters.\nThe caves Don’t leave without a swim inside the Swallows Cave and/or a dive into Mariners Cave.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 84 / 03. Local Information Fiji Local Information Dialling Code: +679 Time zone: UTC +12 Currency: Fijian Dollar (FJD) Emergency: 911 Language: Fijian and English Useful Websites: southpacificislands.travel fiji.travel fiji-savusavu.com/ Entry Formalities Special clearance procedures into Fiji are arranged specifically for the World ARC fleet on Vanua Balavu, in the Lau Group, with the assistance of an Agent. Participants complete advance arrival notification in Vava’u before cruising independently to clear in (to Fiji) at Vanua Balavu. As this is not a Port of Entry, clearance officials are flown in specially to be on hand for a limited period. For those not clearing in within this arranged World ARC period, stopping in the Lau Group is not allowed and yachts must make their first landfall in Fiji at an official Port of Entry to conduct their international clearances independently. The ports of entry to use are: Savusavu (Nawi Island Marina, Copra Shed Marina), Suva, Levuka on Ovalau Island, Port Denarau Marina, Vuda Point for Lautoka. If not making landfall at Vanua Balavu the choice of which port of entry to use will depend on your cruising plans for exploring Fiji. Savusavu and Nawi Marina on the northeastern island of Vanua Levu is a bit further north but makes a logical start point for extensively cruising Fiji. If the time available is more constrained, completing clearance at Ovalau or Suva D A. Savusavu: 16°45’59.85”S, 179°18’35.80”E B. Suva: 18° 7’48.08”S, 178°23’57.29”E C. Levuka: 17°40’50.99”S, 178°51’21.73”E D. Lautoka: 17°36’30.61”S, 177°26’18.63”E B C A Ports of Entry General The Fijian archipelago consists of over 300 islands grouped around the two main islands of Viti Levu and Vanua Levu. Human settlement goes back at least 3,500 years with a mixed Melanesian and Polynesian population supplemented in more recent colonial times by Indian labourers, resulting in a sizeable ethnic Indian population on the two large islands. Traditional Fijian culture survives strongly in the outlying islands where the correct etiquette should always be observed (see later notes).\nWith a delightful mix of mountainous tropical rainforests, pristine beaches and fabulous coral reefs, Fiji has become a popular holiday destination that has much to offer. However, with its widely scattered islands and remote village communities, it is still perfectly possible to avoid the tourist resorts and encounter the true Fijian way of life, especially when cruising.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 85 © World Cruising Club 2026 makes most sense, before continuing down either the North or South coast of Viti Levu. Completing clearances in Lautoka in the West of Viti Levu is convenient for crews who need to make up time or need to leave the yacht in one place for a longer period of time in order to leave it to fly elsewhere or get work done at Vuda Point or Denarau marinas. Regardless of where a yacht is cleared into the country, the procedure will be similar and is outlined below. Local berthing providers can also advise on the process and even act on your behalf (for a fee) to smooth the formalities requirements. These points of contact are Copra Shed Marina in Savusavu, Nawi Marina in Savusavu, the Royal Suva Yacht Club in Suva and Yacht Help Fiji in Port Denarau or Vuda Point Marina for Lautoka.\nVisas A free visa valid for four months is given on arrival to citizens of more than 100 countries, including most of Europe, North and South America. See immigration.gov.fj for a full list. Passports must be valid for more than 6 months longer than intended stay.\nAdvanced Notification Fiji Customs, Bio Security (BAF) and Health departments require advanced notification and information before your arrival. Skippers will be responsible for sending these to the authorities but the forms (where applicable) and contact details will be provided by Rally Control in advance. An automated ‘Read Receipt’ should be kept for your records.\nClearance Procedures The health officer is normally the first official you can expect onboard but if on a mooring or at anchor, they will probably be accompanied by the other officials. A health practique fee will need to be paid, but not necessarily at the same time. Customs and Immigration officials will then board the yacht or you should visit their offices. An inspection of dutiable goods (beer, wine, spirits, tobacco) held onboard should be expected. Duty will be payable on excess quantities of dutiable items. On arrival, it’s recommended to ask locally for advice about current procedures. Overtime will be charged on out-of-hours clearances Immigration If you have not been visited by an Immigration Officer on arrival you will need to visit the Immigration office. In Savusavu the immigration office is about 200 metres to the west of Copra Shed Marina, just before the BP service station. Internal Clearances Fiji used to require internal customs clearances when moving between the areas of different ports of entry but this system has now been abolished. Skippers must report their location once a week to yachtsreport@ frca.org.fj Cruising Permits PLEASE APPLY FOR A CRUISING PERMIT ON THE DAY YOU ARRIVE.\nPermits from the ‘Fijian Affairs Board’ are available through the Marina offices or an Agent for an administration cost of around FJ$100 and this is normally valid for 6 months. The permits are usually issued on the same day of application. You need a permit before you go cruising. World Cruising Club will provide this if you clear in the Lau Group International Clearances Out To exit the country, customs & immigration officers from Lautoka or Denarau will be invited to Musket Cove Resort in order to complete international clearances. If this is not possible, you will be advised by Rally Control and yachts may need to visit Denarau or Lautoka (Vuda Point Marina) to clear out before moving to Musket Cove.\nCrew arriving by air It is important to note that visitors or crew coming into Fiji are not able to enter the country on a one-way ticket. They must have: A) A return ticket OR B) A letter of approval from the Department of Immigration or Fijian Consulate stating that they are authorized to enter on a oneway airline ticket in order to join a yacht and depart by sea. One-way Letters are obtainable through yacht agents or from Fiji Immigration directly. Airlines will not allow crew members travelling on a one way ticket without this letter, and so advanced Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 86 / 03. Local Information Fijian Village Etiquette When visiting a Fijian village it is customary to present a gift (sevusevu) of yaqona (kava), to the Turaga ni Koro (the Village Chief). The presentation is usually in his house and will generally be attended by some of the older men who happen to be in the vicinity at the time, and can quickly turn into a social occasion. Dress modestly. Don’t wear shorts, and women must not wear low cut tops or have bare shoulders. A sulu (sarong, lavalava, pareu) is a useful way to quickly cover up, even for men.\nDo not wear hats or touch anyone’s head, both of which are signs of disrespect. Always remove your shoes before entering any house or other building. Remove any backpacks from your back. Carry in your hands when entering the main village or anywhere near a Village Chief or his home.\nStay with your assigned host. If other villagers ask you to eat or accompany them, politely note that you are with your host and would be honoured to visit with them at some other time. Speak softly; Raised voices are interpreted as expressing anger.\nShipping Shipping ‘Yacht In Transit’ to Fiji is usually duty free provided that the parts are for the repair/ maintenance of the vessel but there are exceptions and rules change so please check.\nPlease contact rally control for the courier shipping address.\nIf a telephone number is needed use: +679 675 0911 Ensure parcel is labelled as “Yacht Parts in Transit”.\nVanua Balavu Anchorage Photo Credit: Clark Davis preparation of this document is important.\nDocuments needed to apply for the letter include: ● Photocopy of the crew(s) passport ● Copy of the flight ticket or complete flight details ● A Request letter from the skipper. If you are the vessel owner, you will not need this letter to re-join your vessel.\nForms and more info at: immigration.gov.fj/ travel-requirements Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Waitui Marina showers, laundry, rubbish disposal, water, help with clearance, upstairs bar, book swap, free dinghy dock. Mooring buoys are orange.\nCopra Shed Marina has moorings buoys (white) and six finger berths, showers, fuel and water. Restaurants, bar, chandlery, dive shop, internet cafe, travel agency and apartments. Dolly Singh is the friendly and helpful manager. Nawi Island Marina A 132 berth marina (opened in 2023) and development with washrooms, laundry, rubbish disposal, water, and electricity. There is a regular shuttle boat to and from the Savusavu waterfront.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 88 / 03. Local Information / 89 © World Cruising Club 2026 Travel Information Airports Savusavu (SVU) Labasa (LBS) Both airports have connections to the rest of Fiji. Savusavu is 10 minutes from the docks and Labasa is 50km away.\nAirlines Fiji Airways fijiairways.com Operates three times a day between Savusavu and Nadi (NAD) and once a day to and from Suva (SUV). They fly around 3 times a day between Labasa and both Nadi and Suva.\nBuses The bus station is 500m from the Copra Shed Marina. Buses go to Labasa or in the other direction to Vatavoni to connect with a ferry service to Taveuni.\nFerries There are several ferry services which connect Savusavu with Taveuni and Suva. The quality of the vessels is only satisfactory.\nSouth Sea Cruises southseacruisesfiji.com Operate ferries from Denaerau through the Mamanuca’s and up to the top of the Yasawa Group.\nMalolo Cat malolocat.com Ferry operator that runs services from Denarau to the Malolo sub-group. Ideal for crew joining / leaving from Musket Cove. Book directly with Musket Cove.\nPatterson Brothers +679 8037204 Operates daily sailings in their cargo, vehicle and passenger ferries between Savusavu & Suva. Goundar Shipping goundarshipping@kidanet.com.fj +679 330 1035 Has a series of ships known as the Lomaiviti Princess. These cargo, vehicle and passenger ferries, operate frequent services between Suva and Vanua Levu. Some ships are more upgraded than others. Interlink Shipping Line operations@interlinkshipping.com.fj +679 893 7746 MV Ohana, which is a more modern and faster ship sails the daytime runs, while the older MV Westerland does slower nightly trips. Taxis Hailed from outside the docks or ordered at Copra Shed reception.\nDon’t Miss The Interior Fiji’s rainforests have no harmful animals or insects, and because of the mountainous nature of the islands there are numerous streams and waterfalls to explore. One of the most famous is the sacred Bouma Falls on the Island of Taveuni.\nDiving and Snorkeling The region is famous for its vibrant coral reefs offering the entire spectrum of underwater life from sharks, rays and many species of fish, to the vivid rainbow of soft corals, sea stars and myriad shrimp.\nWhitewater Rafting Shoot down mountain rivers in kayaks and inflatable rafts. Specialist travel companies can offer a range of tours, mainly on the larger islands and in co-operation with villages, ensuring tourist income stays in the local economy. The Yasawa Group A chain of around 20 islands northwest of Viti Levu which are famous for crystal blue lagoons and some of the Pacific’s most ethereal beaches. The islands have white sandy beaches and crystalline waters, lush tropical rainforests and volcanic peaks that attract fresh tropical rain. A Church Service Even the most irreligious person will be moved by the passion and singing at a Fijian Sunday service. Visitors are always welcome, but dress appropriately.\nLearning some Fijian Whilst English is widely spoken, a few words of Fijian will help make instant friends. Try these: Hello ~ Bula and Thank you ~ vinaka [vee naka].\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 90 / 03. Local Information Fiji: Suva A. Royal Suva Yacht Club Royal Suva Yache Club, P.O. Box 335, Club House, Korovou, Suva, Fiji Islands.\nLocation:\t18° 8’56”S, 178°23’39”E VHF: Ch.16 T: +679 3312921 Website: fijimarinas.com/royal-suva-yacht-club-rsyc Email: rsyc@kidanet.net.fj Water: Potable B. Anchorage A B Mooring The Royal Suva Yacht Club is located in Korovou, in the northern outskirts of the city. Fees are FJ$50 per night for an alongside berth, when available. The depth is around 2m. The alternative is to anchor. The holding in the anchorage area is patchy and there is debris on the sea bed. There is a charge of FJ$5 per person per day for using the RSYC dinghy dock.\nAn alternative anchorage is off the nearby Tradewinds Hotel.\nWater There is water on the pontoons at the Royal Suva Yacht Club.\nClearance Contact Suva Port Control on VHF Ch 16 on arrival, who immediately advise new arrivals to contact the Royal Suva Yacht Club (RSYC). The club will arrange for the various agencies to visit the boat. Crew must not come ashore until cleared. The Yacht Club’s boat will transport the officers to clear the waiting yacht(s) in the quarantine area. The club charges FJ$50 for all the transport fees. There are two daily clearance times: Mon-Fri 09:00 & 15:00. Yachts arriving after 15:00 on Fridays will have to wait until the next normal working day, or agree to pay the extra charges.\nA to Z of Boat Services Boatyards There are no yards designed to haul out yachts. There are options in the yards servicing commercial vessels, but these should be for emergencies only. Catamarans can dry out on the RSYC slipway for antifouling, and this is one of the few places in Fiji that can accommodate catamarans. Marine Industrial & Structural (MIS) Engineering Limited kelemis@connect.com.fj +679 3312938/ +679 3316378 Located close to the yacht club, several slipways capable of handling most kinds of craft, haul out up to 100 tons and repairs: welding, machining, fabrication. Chandlery Carpenters Marine +679 3313644 A good range of outboard engines, generators, dinghies, pumps, etc. Agents for Johnson and Evinrude. Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. A well stocked chandlery.\nCharts Carpenters Shipping carpenters.com.fj Stockist of British, Australian and Fijian charts and publications. Admiralty Chart Agent.\nHydrographic Office +679 3315457 Amra Street, Walu Bay. Stockist of local charts and nautical publications. Electrical and Electronics Bayside Electrical Ltd +679 3361510 Wailada, Lami Clay Energy clayenergy.com.fj Suppliers and repairers of wind and solar power systems. Cooke’s United kat_mawi@hotmail.com +679 3303845/ Between RSYC and Suva town. +679 9924175 Rizvy S. Ali +679 3387361/+679 9942616 Universal Electronics +679 312808 31 Knolly Street.\nFibreglass & GRP Repair Pacific Fibreglass Ltd +679 3361613 The Fibreglass Shop +679 3361057 Fuel Shell fuel station at the RSYC. 2m depth at high tide.\nGas (Cooking) Fiji Gas +679 332435 Amra Street, Walu Bay. Will fill your tanks while you wait. Mechanical Asco Motors +679 3384888 Ratu Mara Road, Nabua . Yamaha and Mariner agents. Carpenters Motors +679 3318538 Argo Street, Walu Bay. Tohatsu and Honda agents. Local Information Doctors / Hospitals Emergency: 911 PSH Hospitals in Nadi* (Viti Levu Isl) Aspen Medical in Lautoka* (Viti Levu Isl) Colonial War Memorial Hospital in Suva (Viti Levu Isl) Nasese Private Hospital in Suva (Viti Levu Isl) The primary hospitals with a hyperbaric chambers in Fiji are PSH Hospitals* and Lautoka* A to Z of What’s on shore Internet At the RSYC, WiFi available covering the anchorage.\nLaundry At RSYC or several places in town.\nMoney ATMs in Downtown Suva. Machines nearer the RSYC may charge for a withdrawal.\nShopping & Supermarket Don’t miss a visit to the Municipal Market which has an excellent selection of local fresh produce. Open daily.\nThere are several well stocked supermarkets including: New World Foods newworld.com.fj MH supermarkets mh.com.fj Cost-U-less (Costco subsidiary) costuless.com Yacht Club Royal Suva Yacht Club (RSYC) Close to Suva Concrete docks, dinghy dock, two cradles and a slipway. The club will hold mail for incoming yachts. Facilities include a restaurant and bar (cash only), BBQ, book exchange, water, fuel (cash only), money exchange, ice, laundry, showers and a chandelry. Travel Information Airports Suva Nausori (SUV) nausoriairport.com 20km away from Suva, the capital of Fiji and the RSYC. It has connections to the rest of Fiji with multiple flights daily to and from Nadi International Airport.\nAirlines Fiji Airways fijiairways.com Operate several schedules daily around Fiji.\nBuses Minivans and buses, in and out of the city centre, pass the RSYC frequently during the day.\nFerries There are several ferry services which connect to Savusavu with Taveuni. See page 89.\nTaxis Can be hailed from outside the RSYC or ordered through the yacht club office.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Vuda Marina Vuda Marina, PO Box 5717, Lautoka, Fiji Islands Location:\t17°40’55”S, 177°23’8”E VHF: Ch.16 T: +679 666 8214 Website: vudamarina.com.fj Email: info@vudamarina.com.fj Water: Potable Power: 240V, 10amp B. Port Denarau Marina Location:\t17°46’14”S, 177°22’54”E VHF: Ch.14 T: +679 675 0600 Website: denaraumarina.com Email: reservations@denaraumarina.com Water: Potable Power: 240V, 32amp C. Nadi International Airport D. Musket Cove A B Mooring There are two berthing options in the Nadi Bay area. Either at the Port Denarau Marina or Vuda Point Marina at the northern end of Nadi Bay. Both places offer good options for boat projects or leaving the boat securely. Port Denarau is the most accessible for Nadi Town and sits in an area developed for shopping and hotels. Water All water at Vuda Marina is potable. Each dock has a shared freshwater tap and there is freshwater available at the fuel depot. Potable fresh water also available at Port Denarau marina.\nC D Clearance If you sail directly to Nadi Bay from Tonga you will need to clear in to Fiji independently. Both Vuda Marina and Denarau Marina are ports of entry for Fiji. Send in your Advanced Notification Form at least 48 hours prior to your arrival - it is best to submit prior to leaving Tonga. Customs Clearance facilities are available from 8am to 4pm Monday to Friday only. Weekend and Public Holiday clearances are permitted, however overtime fees will be charged by the relevant government agencies. Please ensure you are aware of the Biosecurity clearance procedures and have provisioned appropriately. See useful information on the Denarau Marina website: denaraumarina.com/ arriving-departing-fiji Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 94 / 03. Vuda Point Marina vudamarina.com.fj +679 666 8214 Largest travel lift in Fiji capable of hauling out up to 63 tonnes, with a maximum beam of 5.85m. Local Information / 95 © World Cruising Club 2026 A to Z of What’s on Shore Dentist Dr Uma Sharma Namaka drumasharma@connect.com.fj +679 6721999 ByHospital supplies Internet/Mobile Both Denarau and Vuda Point Marina’s have WiFi.\nAll customers of Vuda Marina are entitled to complimentary one-hour high-speed internet per day. Daily and weekly passes are also available at very affordable prices. A 24-hour pass is FJ$5 and a weekly pass is FJ$20. Vodafone has roaming agreements with Australia, NZ and UK. Digicel provides a reliable network connection in most areas.\nLaundry Hiralal Laundry +679 6661972 24 Veve Street (by market), Lautoka. Money Banks throughout town (ANZ and Westpac).\nPharmacy Island Chemist High Street, Nadi - can supply antimalarials without prescription.\nShopping Markets Market in Lautoka. Nadi Market will ship to Musket Cove.\nFreddy’s Marketing freddysmarketing@connect.com.fj Has selection of imported fruit and vegetables. Fiji Meats facebook.com/fijimeatslimited +679 6660255 Branches in Savusavu and Suva. Order there for collection in Lautoka (close to wharf). Will freeze and vacuum pack if ordered in advance. Supermarkets Victoria Wines and Spirits Nadi Town info@victoriawines.com.fj Can deliver to Musket Cove, or Lautoka or Denarau. Good selection of wines and spirits (expensive in Fiji). Morris Hedstrom (known as MH), in Nadi/Lautoka and throughout Fiji. Travel Information Airports Nadi International Airport (NAN) airportsfiji.com Fiji’s main international gateway, located a short distance away from Denarau Marina.\nAirlines International airlines flying into Fiji: Fiji Airways fijiairways.com Qantas qantas.com.au Air New Zealand .airnewzealand.com Virgin Australia virginaustralia.com Ferries There are ferry services operating fast catamarans and ships between all of the island groups in Fiji. Suva is the main port. See page 89. Buses Pacific Transport operate buses between Suva and Lautoka, stopping in most villages. Taxis Easy to find on the street. Should be metered. Licenced cabs have “LT” on yellow number plates.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 96 / 03. Local Information Fiji: Musket Cove, Malolo LaiLai Musket Cove Yacht Club Mailing address: Private Mail Bag NAP 0352, Nadi Airport, Fiji Islands Location:\t17°46’15.94”S, 177°11’31.23”E VHF: Ch.68 T: (679) 666 2215 ext 253 Website: musketcovefiji.com Email: mcyc@musketcovefiji.com Water: Potable rain water Musket Cove is primarily a holiday resort, on Malolo Lailai Island, with a small marina serving visiting yachts. There are no major yacht services at Musket Cove and everything should be done before arrival. Special arrangements may be in place for the visit of World ARC. A greater selection of services are available at Denarau Marina, which is on the way to Malolo Lailai Island.\nClearance Yachts must clear in to Fiji before they arrive at Musket Cove. It is suggested that yachts clearing in to Fiji at Savusavu use Copra Shed Marina in the centre of town. Alternatively both Vuda Marina and Denarau Marina in Nadi Bay are ports of entry for Fiji. Please ensure you are aware of the Biosecurity clearance procedures and have provisioned appropriately. See useful information on the Denaru Marina website: denaraumarina.com/ arriving-departing-fiji World ARC will arrange for the departure clearance of yachts from Musket Cove Island Resort & Marina.\nMooring Musket Cove Resort maintains 24 mooring buoys off the entrance to the marina lagoon area, and a large dock to which yachts moor Med-style by anchor, with their stern or bow to the dock (where it is hoped the majority of World ARC yachts will be berthed). Check availability on arrival. The entrance to the area where the marina is situated is limited by draft. There is a depth of 2m at Low Water, with a height of tide of approximately 1.5m. Yachts should contact Musket Cove Marina before entering on Ch 68.\nWater Collected potable rainwater is available (approx. FJ$0.10 per litre), but possibly not large quantities. Fill tanks before arrival, or make water at anchor in the bay, or after departure. Contact Marina Office. Bottled water is available from General Trader Store.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 97 © World Cruising Club 2026 A to Z of Boat Services Very limited facilities for repair or equipment purchase in Musket Cove. See services available in nearby Nadi Bay.\nChandlery Trader General Store In the resort are able to have items shipped in duty free. Limited range in stock, including bottled gas. Chandleries in Denarau Marina Electricity Occasionally available through 20 pontoon power pedestals. All 240v; limited 3 phase power outlets; standard European plug. Cost FJ$0.60/KwH. Fuel Limited quantity of diesel and petrol available. Costs about FJ$0.20 more than Port Denarau. Mon-Sun 08:00-17:00. Advise Marina Office before moving to the fuel dock.\nGas (Cooking) Propane is available through the Trader General Store.\nHardware Store Trader General Store In the resort has limited supplies.\nA to Z of What’s on Shore Accommodation Musket Cove Island Resort & Marina musketcovefiji.com Ask for discounted World ARC rates. Book Exchange Book swap at the marina and resort offices.\nDiving Subsurface Fiji fijidiving.com Based at Musket Cove resort, can meet all your diving needs, including tank fills. Hairdressers Ask at Mandra Spa.\nIce Available at the Trader General Store in the resort.\nInternet/Mobile WiFi is available throughout the resort. Purchase an access voucher for 30 minutes to 7 days at Reception. 7 days access costs approx. FJ$50. Local SIM cards available from the shop.\nLaundry There is a self-service token-operated laundry opposite the Trader Cafe. Washers and dryers. Please ask the staff at the shop to set the machines going, FJ$10 per wash or dry. Alternatively, there are service wash facilities organised via the marina office. Mail Mail is delivered to the marina office at about 14:00 daily (except Sunday). Leave outgoing mail in the blue sack at the resort mail box at reception, where stamps can be purchased (cash only). Yachts are given a “Rotation” number on clearing into Fiji which must be declared on shipping documents for Duty Free in transit. There may still be duty payable and parcels may incur a transfer fee from Leeward Island Services (operators of Malolo Catamaran ferry) or the Fiji Post Office, which will be passed to the recipient for payment. Best agents are DHL and FEDEX.\nMoney There’s an ATM near the reception at Musket Cove Resort. Yachts may open an account at the resort. Money exchange is available in small quantities (US$100) through the resort reception.\nYou only need cash for stamps (at the boutique) and mobile phone top-ups. More than one account can be set-up per boat. Payment can be made with cards throughout the resort, a surcharge may apply.\nPharmacy Trader Shop General Store and Resort Boutique (situated close to the resort reception), both have limited stock.\nPost Office Resort Boutique sells stamps. Marina Office may be able to help with any courier services required.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 98 / 03. Local Information Restaurants Musket Cove Yacht Club Island Bar A favourite amongst visiting crews & great to watch the sunset whilst having your own BBQ on the many BBQ pits. Order your BBQ packs from the Coffee Cove Café before 16:00. Pizza ordering for delivery to the Coffee Cove also available. Open 10:00 until late.\nDick’s Place Bar & Bistro Main restaurant of the resort. Open from 07:30 for breakfast, last orders for dinner are 21:30. Snack menu available 07:30-17:00. Shirt and dry shorts required. Mobile phone free area.\nThe Coffee Cove Café Situated behind the Trader Shop. Relaxing place tucked away for a light snack, coffee or iced drink from 10:00-18:30.\nPlantation Resort and Lomani Resort Venture along the coast road past the runway to the next resorts as an alternative for an evening out.\nRubbish Bins are located to the right of the marina office. All rubbish is to be bagged; recycle using white bins for glass bottles, and the sack for plastic bottles.\nShowers and Toilets Marina block available. Visiting yachts are requested not to use their shower and toilet facilities on board whilst in the marina, and not to pump out holding tanks until out past the reef.\nShopping Trader General Store Has a reasonable selection of foods, including some fresh meats, vegetables, and bread (but is only re-supplied once a week). It is not possible to purchase alcohol from shops on the island only via bars and restaurants. Open: Mon-Sun 08:00-18:00.\nSports Facilities Swimming Pool Visiting yachtsmen are welcome to use the resort swimming pool situated by the bistro. This is a mobile free zone. Whilst open 24 hours it should be noted there is no lifeguard on duty, and guests are not allowed to take their own food or drink to the pool area. Watersports Visit the Dock Activities Bure close to the Marina Office. Golf The neighbouring Plantation Resort has a nine nine hole course, green fees include use of clubs and a trundler.\nYacht Club Known as one of the most exclusive yacht clubs in the Pacific, Musket Cove Yacht Club membership is only open to those who have sailed in from, or are sailing out to, a foreign port. Apply at the Marina office for life membership, for which there is a modest charge.\nTravel Information No transport is required at Musket Cove Resort as everywhere is only a short walk away. The staff use golf buggies around the resort, so you might be offered a lift!\nBicycle Rental Available through Musket Cove resort, book at the Activities Bure. Cost from F$45 per day for adults and F$25 per day for children.\nFerries Malolo Cat Operates between Port Denarau and Malolo Lailai Island, crossing in 50 minutes. It is a good way for new crew to join, or to do some shopping in Nadi or Lautoka, or simply go sightseeing on the mainland. Fares are approx. FJ$100 105 one way, FJ$190 return. The fare includes coach transfer to/from Nadi airport and Nadi Hotels. Departs Musket Cove at 05:45 08:45, 12:15 and 15:30. Departs Denarau at 07:30, 10:30, 14:00 and 17:00. Book 24 hours before travel online or visit reception. Yacht Club members received 50% discount on ferry fares.\nTaxis Book a resort chauffeur buggy via reception.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 99 © World Cruising Club 2026 Vanuatu Local Information Dialling Code: +678 Time zone: UTC +11 Currency: Vatu (VUV / VT) Emergency: Police: +678 22 222 Fire: +678 22 333 Medical: +678 22 100 Language: Bislama, English, French Useful Websites: vanuatu.travel tourismvanuatu.com Entry Formalities Boats are officially cleared into the Vanuatu islands at Port Resolution in Tanna under special arrangements. Customs, Immigration and Quarantine officials make a special all-day visit to the Yacht Club for World ARC, enabling skippers to complete necessary paperwork without having to travel to the capital town. Quarantine officials will ask skippers about any fresh produce they have onboard and may decide (at their discretion) to visit the boats at anchor in the bay for a brief inspection. Visas Visitors from all Commonwealth countries, European Union, European Free Trade Area, USA, Brazil and the Russian Federation do not need visas. For a full list of exempt and non-exempt nations visit the Vanuatu Immigration Services website or advice at vanuatu.travel/ en/ Crew Flying into Vanuatu Those flying into the country to meet a yacht and sail out, must submit a ‘One Way Requirement Form’, and a supporting letter from the receiving skipper, ahead of departure to Vanuatu. The letter from the skipper should outline the vessel name and details, give permission for the person to join the crew in Vanuatu and give an undertaking that the crew person listed will be sailing out on that vessel.\nImmigration must be emailed the completed One-Way Requirement form, a copy of the Crew Passport, the Skipper Confirmation Letter, a copy of the flight ticket along with payment for the fee of VT6,000 (per person).\nImmigration will then approve and prepare a ‘One Way Requirement Letter’ which authorises a one-way ticket departing via yacht and send this back to you. This will also be emailed directly to the airlines by Immigration. The crew member should present all documentation to Immigration on arrival as they enter Vanuatu. An alternative option is to buy a refundable return ticket. Local Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 100 / 03. Local Information Clearing out of Vanuatu World ARC boats will be able to clear out with Customs and Immigration from their base in Port Vila. Arrangements are normally made for officials to visit on the morning before departure. General Vanuatu is an archipelago of 83 islands around 1,750km east of Australia, lying at the end of a chain of volcanic peaks stretching southeast from Papua New Guinea. With a unique blend of Melanesian (Ni-Vanuatu) tribal communities, pristine beaches, stunning active volcanoes and truly memorable diving, Vanuatu has much to entice visitors.\nPilotage Information Charts 1494 Efate and Plans Pilots Landfalls of Paradise (Hinz) Pacific Crossing Guide (RCC) South Pacific handbook (Stanley) Cruising the South Pacific (Austin) South Pacific Anchorages (Clay) Port Resolution, on the island of Tanna, is the landfall for yachts arriving from Musket Cove. Yachts will clear Customs, Immigration and quarantine here, with officers brought down by special arrangement. Staff from the local bank should also be on hand on arrival to exchange money. Support ashore will be through the Port Resolution Yacht Club, an organisation set up by the local villagers and run by an enthusiastic man called Werry Narua. Sailing into Port Resolution with the backdrop of the volcano Mount Yasur will be just the start to the magical experience of a visit to Tanna, which means “earth” in Tannese. Mount Yasur is the world’s most accessible active volcano, and being so close to such natural raw power is an awe inspiring experience, especially at sunset, with glowing lumps of lava being thrown into the air. Kastum village & Mount Yasur Photo Credit Christoper Francis Port Resolution, Tanna Location: 19°31’25”S, 169°29’47”E Water: Not Potable Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 101 © World Cruising Club 2026 Port Vila, Efate Yachting World Marina Yachting World Marina, Lini Highway, Port Vila, Vanuatu.\nLocation: 17°44’34.20”S, 168°18’46.10”E VHF: Ch.16 T: +678 232 73 Email: yachting@vanuatu.com.vu Website: yachtingworld-vanuatu.com Water: Potable A tour will be arranged to a Kastom (the rules keeping ancient ancestral legacies alive) village, where villagers perform wearing nambas (penis sheaths) and grass skirts, singing and playing local musical instruments. The tour then continues up to Mount Yasur to experience the volcano at sunset. These will be run over a number of days as the yachts arrive.\nSocial activities will be centred at the Port Resolution Yacht Club, where there will be an Official Welcoming Ceremony, at which it is customary for an exchange of gifts to be made. The World ARC team have asked the village to request a ‘wish list’ of items it needs for the community, which will be made available to participants. Don’t Miss Mount Yasur The world’s most accessible active volcano.\nKastom ceremonies Immerse yourself in the traditional Vanuatu society, with ritual celebrations of different aspects of village life. Remember to bring gifts when attending ceremonies, as many involve ritual exchanges to establish status. Kava drinking Derived from the root of a pepper vine and common throughout Melanesia, drinking kava is the foundation for many traditional ceremonies and its consumption is controlled by strict rules in the tribal areas. On Tanna it is men only! Be warned, the kava found in Vanuatu is the strongest in Melanesia.\nBislama The national language of Vanuatu and a form of pidgin English, with other words added from French and Spanish. There are numerous books available locally, and learning a few words will earn you a reward of many smiles in the markets. See the language page on the Vanuatu Tourism website for a basic introduction to the language.\nMooring As well as a number of mooring buoys, berthing is available on the sea wall, run by Yachting World Marina. There is a 3m draft on the entrance to Port Vila between the two channel buoys. Tidal range is 1.2m. Be aware of the power cables between Port Vila and Iririki Island with an air draft close to the quay of approx. 23m. If in doubt as to mast height, harbour moorings must be approached via the southern end channel of Iririki Island. Yachting World hours (for fuel, laundry and payment) are Mon-Fri 08:00-16:00, Sat 08:00-15:00. Water is available from near the Dinghy Pontoon after hours.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 102 / 03. Local Information A to Z of Boat Services Boatyard, Rigging, Sails and Welding Port Vila Boatyard portvilaboatyard@vanuatu.com.vu +678 57182 facebook.com/portvilaboatyard +67823417 Situated round the far south side of the bay past the Main Wharf. Haulout on a trailer – limit 25 tons. Full range of services available. A good place for a haul-out and anti-foul before entering Australia. All yachts will have sufficient shore power, as long as heavily consumable units are not used. Fuel At the Yachting World dock, next to the berthing quay. Pre-book at the Yachting World Office. Duty free fuel is available on the day directly before departure, with provision of Customs form (available through the a Customs and Immigration office). Depths at LW 2.30m and HW 3.80m.\nGas (Cooking) Gas bottles can be refilled at the petrol station across the road from the Marina (Pacific Fuels). Take in before 8am on Mon, Wed, Fri and they will return on the same day. They can be replaced at Au Bon Marche supermarket. Can also take dinghy to the Gas terminal near the customs dock, they will fill propane bottles whilst you wait, however they cannot do Camping Gaz cylinders.\nCamping Gaz cylinders can be taken to Port Vila Hardware Tagabe - They can fill Camping Gaz cylinders as long as you have an adapter valve to standard 1” thread. World ARC yachts are recommended to stock up on gas before leaving Vanuatu as European and US bottles cannot be refilled in Australia. Basic clean and service of watermakers - no parts kept in stock.\nWater At Yachting World dock or adjacent to the Dinghy Pontoon after hours. An additional small shower block is also situated at the north end of the wall.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information Money The nearest ATM to Yachting World is at the Pacific fuel station across the road, or outside the casino. Across the street from the Grand Hotel is a BSP which offers good exchange rates. ANZ and National Bank of Vanuatu will both exchange currencies in main branches.\nGoodies Forex goodiesforex.com Independent foreign exchange on Lini highway usually providing best rates. Mon-Fri 07:30-16:30, Sat, Sun, and Public Holidays: 07:30-12:00.\nANZ Bank Has a 12-hour currency exchange open 09:00-21:00, and an ATM. Normal banking hours are Mon-Fri 08:30-15:00. Pharmacy Healthwise Pharmacy +678 22707 On Kumul Highway. Drug Store On Lini Highway. Well stocked and helpful pharmacist. Mon-Fri 0730-1800; Sat 07:30-12:00; Sun 08:30-12:00. There is also a dispensary at the Hospital. Post Office Lini Highway. Mon-Fri 07:30-16:30; Sat 07:30-11:30. Public Holidays and when there is a cruise ship in port: 07:30-15:00.\nRubbish Rubbish (for Marina Customers) may be left within the rubbish enclosure at the rear of the restaurant behind the Marina.\nShopping Markets Excellent fresh produce market on main street. Open round the clock from early Monday through to 12 noon on Saturday. Prices are rock bottom and are clearly marked; there is no bargaining. Venture to the back where there are benches for diners to enjoy interesting freshly cooked dishes.\nSupermarkets Au Bon Marche aubonmarche.co Excellent well stocked supermarket with a good selection of meats, fish and fresh bread. 5 minutes out of town by taxi or a 10-minute walk. Mon-Sun 06:00-19:30. There are several located around Port Vila, go North along the Harbour road to find the best one. A smaller, ‘express’ Au Bon Marche is a three-minute walk from the Marina.\nDockside Duty Free Wines and Spirits tusker@vanuatu.com.vu Lini Highway. Opposite Yachting World. Open Mon-Sat. Sports Facilities Tennis, golf and various field sports are available. Telephones Local SIM cards available from the airport or shops in Port Vila. The two main providers are Digicel and Vodafone, both work in most areas.\nTourist Information Vanuatu Tourism Office vanuatu.travel Don’t Miss Diving Take a trip to some of the world’s most renowned dive sites; Million Dollar Point and the liner SS President Coolidge.\nExtreme sports Traditional creeper jumping began in Pentecost Island, Vanuatu, and was the inspiration for modern bungee jumping, so why not explore your extreme side with some adrenaline rushing activities?\nEating out Vila residents like to call their home the ‘gourmet capital of the South Pacific’ and after several months cruising in remote locations you may well agree. The diverse ethnic and cultural mix of Vila is reflected in food styles served in over forty restaurants and cafés - French, Vietnamese, Chinese, Japanese, Italian, Mexican, Thai, Melanesian and Mediterranean. A few suggestions: Casa Mia, Stone Grill, Taste of India, The Boat House, L’Houstalet, Moorings Seaside Bar & Bistro, Pizza Hot AU Far.\nLocal Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information Local Information Dialling Code: +61 State Codes +07 for Queensland +08 for Northern Territory Time zone: UTC +10 Currency: Australian $ Emergency: 000 Language: English Useful Websites: australia.com queensland.com.au tourismtopend.com.au mackayregion.com Australia Entry Formalities Mackay is a Port of Entry into Australia. Representatives of the Australian Biosecurity, Customs and Border Force will be available on site. World ARC yachts will be directed to a dedicated pontoon on arrival to clear in. Crews are not allowed to leave the boat until cleared in. The quarantine pontoon is gated and locked to prevent unauthorised shore access. Yachts arriving by night should be prepared to have to wait until the next day before being permitted ashore. Exclusively for World ARC, the authorities draft in extra staff to assist so daytime clearances are usually not further delayed by weekend or Bank Holiday arrivals.\nVisas It is essential that individual crew members apply for a temporary visitors’ visa online at www.homeaffairs.gov.au/Trav/Visa-1 before departure from Vanuatu. Failure to do so will result in a fine and crews not being allowed to leave the yacht on arrival. This also applies for crews arriving by air into Australia.\nIf the yacht’s registered Owner or Skipper is arriving in Australia on the boat, but not leaving with it, they will need to leave a signed letter of authorisation for whoever is taking over responsibility of the boat. Mackay Marina Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 107 © World Cruising Club 2026 Australian Quarantine Requirements Australia has some of the strictest laws in the world regarding the importation of food, liquids, souvenirs and other items. Whilst friendly and informative, the Quarantine Inspection Service team in Mackay are very thorough, and it’s best to prepare as much as possible to avoid having items removed from your boat unnecessarily. As the regulations are constantly changing you will be given a brief in Vanuatu detailing prohibited items. You can also follow the guidelines below, to enable you to provision correctly and manage your food stocks well in advance (noting that the actual regulations may change slightly before your actual arrival): Prohibited items: ● All fresh and non-commercially packaged food items. This includes all meats (fresh, frozen or pre-cooked from fresh), fruits and vegetables ● Most dairy products, including opened jars of mayonnaise ● Honey containing honeycomb ● All living plants and herbs ● All other dry stores, open or unopened, will be inspected to ensure that they are free of insects. If free of insects, they will not be removed. ● Many other items will be inspected, including all souvenir-type items. If products are found to contain signs of life, they will either be treated on the spot, or removed for destruction.\nItems allowed: ● Meat from Australia or New Zealand that is commercially packaged & the country of origin easily identified. ● All Fish ● Commercially packaged products from a prescribes list of FMD Approved countries (will be confirmed in Vanuatu). ● All alcohol. Bottles may be liable for inspection but will not be removed. Tips for ensuring a quick clearance: ● Have all food, drink and souvenir items easily accessible for inspection ● Prepare a list of all your provisions to show Biosecurity ● Thoroughly clean all diving and fishing gear before arrival ● If unsure, declare it and put it aside for inspection.\nAntifouling You may be asked questions such as when and where the last antifouling was carried out, and the type of products used. The authorities appreciate that yachts on World ARC are constantly on the move, so do not generally present a problem with regards to build up of biomatter on their hulls, although it is recommended that you clean the waterline before departure from Vanuatu.\nFirearms and weapons Firearms and ammunition need to be declared before arrival into Australia. On arrival, Customs agents will remove firearms for safe storage during your stay. You then need to notify Customs one week before you plan to leave the country, and they will arrange for your firearms to be sent (at their cost) to your port of departure, for collection when clearing out of Australia. Flare guns, parachute flares and spear guns are not classified as firearms by Australian Customs.\nLocal Information Rally Handbook World Cruising Club © World Cruising Club 2026 108 / 03. Local Information Immigration The World ARC team pre-notify the authorities of your impending arrival so yachts are not required to provide the standard 96-hour notification of arrival normally required by Australian Immigration. This does mean, however, that yachts should not be tempted to stop, even briefly, at any of the off-lying islands on their way into Mackay from the Hydrographer’s Passage as this will cause later complications with the authorities who track incoming World ARC vessels’ progress closely to speed up your reception process. Tourist Refund Scheme (TRS) Australia currently offers a Tourist Refund Scheme (TRS), which allows temporary visitors to claim back the GST paid on goods bought during their stay. Note this is for goods only and excludes services and labour.\nMost items are eligible for refund, including clothing, jewellery, shoes, cameras, small electrical items, souvenirs, and wine. Beer, spirits and tobacco products, as well as consumed or hazardous goods are not eligible for a GST refund. To claim a refund, you must meet the following conditions: ● Spend AUS$300 or more (including GST) in one store ● Purchase goods no more than 30 days before your departure ● Hold a tax invoice (provided by the retailer) ● Have the goods with you when you leave Australia (hand luggage if departing by air) TRS refunds will be processed by Australian Border Force during the outbound clearances in Darwin. If you are leaving the country by air, all international airports have TRS facilities to help you with your refund on departure. Refunds are normally processed onto a credit card.\nNote that GST cannot be claimed back on Diesel. It is however possible to claim be ‘excise’ (approximately 9%) via a separate form that will be provided to you in Darwin.\nImporting spare parts into Australia Goods with a value of less than AU$1000 can be sent via the normal postal system from abroad, and do not require any form of clearance. The sender should attach a contact name and number of the recipient in Australia. Parts over AUS$1000 in value will be subject to additional clearance procedures and normally tax will be paid. Visiting yachts though can normally avoid paying tax by addressing parcels to ‘Yacht in transit’ but the process can be complicated and time consuming. It is a good idea to send goods only from a supplier with experience of sending parts to Australia. A customs broker in Australia may be required to complete a Temporary Import Declaration. More details can be obtained from www.border.gov.au Temporary Import of yachts into Australia Previous World ARC yachts registered in Australia that have not already paid GST (Goods & Service Tax) on their boat purchase have been able to avoid paying expensive import taxes by temporarily importing their yachts into Australia for the time that the rally is in the country. Up to date information from the Australian Government can be found here: www.abf.gov.au/importing-exporting-and-manufacturing/temporary-imports This temporary importation process can be difficult to understand, previous World ARC yachts have used Joel Kenny from Aurora Yacht Logistics for this service. Aurora is the exclusive Australian agent for World Cruising Club Corporate Members Peters & May.\nJoel Kenny +61 07 39005900/ yacht-transport.com.au / joel@auroralogistics.com Local Information World ARC World Cruising Club 03. Local Information / 113 © World Cruising Club 2026 Don’t Miss: In Mackay Mackay Regional Botanical Gardens: The Botanical Garden showcases the flora of the central coasts bio-region. The garden is a must see whilst visiting Mackay. You can take a leisurely stroll around the Garden’s walking tracks. Bluewater Lagoon: A free, family-friendly attraction featuring three tiered lagoons, an interactive children’s water playground with drop bucket & water slide, barbecue facilities & café onsite.\nBeaches: The Mackay region has over 31 beautiful beaches. That’s a different beach for each day of the month!\nHeritage walks: Mackay has a rich history, which is still evident today in its remaining historic buildings, memorials, cemeteries and other historical sites. A brochure providing a self-guided heritage walk through the city is available from Tourist Information Services and the Mackay City Council Building in Gordon Street. Don’t Miss: Beyond Mackay Historic Greenmount Homestead at Walkerston was the first property in the district and offers visitors a taste of years gone by. Open: 09:30-12:30. To get to Greenmount Homestead travel from Mackay on the Peak Downs Highway, to Walkertson and Greenmount Homestead. It is located about five kilometres west of Walkertson.\nHibiscus Coast The Hibiscus Coast district is located about 30 minutes to the north of Mackay city and features the natural beauty of Cape Hillsborough National Park along with wonderful seaside townships such as Seaforth, Halliday Bay and Ball Bay. Rainforest Eungella (pronounced ‘Yungulla’) National Park about 60 minutes’ drive from Mackay city, covering 51,700 hectares and boasts Australia’s largest stretch of sub-tropical rainforest. It has over 20 kilometres of walking tracks, which feature spectacular views. This National Park is famous for its population of platypus, which are frequently seen in the waterways around Broken River. National Parks Mackay is located within proximity to a number of beautiful National Parks. These include Eungella National Park, Cape Hillsborough, Cape Palmerston and Keswick, Carlisle, Brampton and Scawfell Islands. Further details can be obtained from the Queensland Parks and Wildlife Services.\nThursday Island is an interesting place to visit, and a friendly place to stop and explore. Careful navigation is required through the Torres Straits, with the strong currents and many reefs. Important: Australian Fisheries may consider yachts that have been on the hard for over 5 days in Australia and afterwards stopped in any of the islands on Torres Strait as an international arrival and will require a full inspection on arrival in Darwin.
